# Apollo

Greek god of music, prophecy and healing

This article is about the Greek and Roman god. For the spaceflight program, see [Apollo program](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apollo_program "Apollo program"). For other uses, see [Apollo (disambiguation)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apollo_\(disambiguation\) "Apollo (disambiguation)").

"Thyraeus" redirects here. For other uses, see [Thyraeus (mythology)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thyraeus_\(mythology\) "Thyraeus (mythology)").

"Phoebus" redirects here. Not to be confused with [Phobos (mythology)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Phobos_\(mythology\) "Phobos (mythology)"). For other uses, see [Phoebus (disambiguation)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Phoebus_\(disambiguation\) "Phoebus (disambiguation)").

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/75/Apollo%2C_God_of_Light%2C_Eloquence%2C_Poetry_and_the_Fine_Arts_with_Urania%2C_Muse_of_Astronomy_-_Charles_Meynier.jpg/220px-Apollo%2C_God_of_Light%2C_Eloquence%2C_Poetry_and_the_Fine_Arts_with_Urania%2C_Muse_of_Astronomy_-_Charles_Meynier.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Apollo,_God_of_Light,_Eloquence,_Poetry_and_the_Fine_Arts_with_Urania,_Muse_of_Astronomy_-_Charles_Meynier.jpg)

*Apollo, God of Light, Eloquence, Poetry and the Fine Arts with Urania, Muse of Astronomy* (1798) by [Charles Meynier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charles_Meynier "Charles Meynier")

**Apollo**[\[a\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-2) is one of the [Olympian deities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Twelve_Olympians "Twelve Olympians") in [ancient Greek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ancient_Greek_religion "Ancient Greek religion") and [Roman religion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ancient_Roman_religion "Ancient Roman religion") and [Greek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greek_mythology "Greek mythology") and [Roman mythology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman_mythology "Roman mythology"). Apollo has been recognized as a god of archery, music and dance, truth and prophecy, healing and diseases, the [Sun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sun "Sun") and light, poetry, and more. One of the most important and complex of the Greek gods, he is the son of [Zeus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zeus "Zeus") and [Leto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leto "Leto"), and the twin brother of [Artemis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Artemis "Artemis"), goddess of the hunt. He is considered to be the most beautiful god and is represented as the ideal of the *[kouros](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kouros "Kouros")* (ephebe, or a beardless, athletic youth). Apollo is known in Greek-influenced [Etruscan mythology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Etruscan_mythology "Etruscan mythology") as *[Apulu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apulu "Apulu")*.[\[2\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-3)

As the patron deity of [Delphi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delphi "Delphi") (*Apollo Pythios*), Apollo is an [oracular](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oracular "Oracular") god—the prophetic [deity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deity "Deity") of the [Delphic Oracle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pythia "Pythia") and also the deity of ritual purification. His oracles were often consulted for guidance in various matters. He was in general seen as the god who affords help and wards off evil, and is referred to as *[Alexicacus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexicacus "Alexicacus")*, the "averter of evil". Medicine and healing are associated with Apollo, whether through the god himself or mediated through his son [Asclepius](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asclepius "Asclepius"). Apollo delivered people from epidemics, yet he is also a god who could bring ill health and deadly [plague](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plague_\(disease\) "Plague (disease)") with his arrows. The invention of archery itself is credited to Apollo and his sister Artemis. Apollo is usually described as carrying a silver or golden bow and a quiver of silver or golden arrows.

As the god of *mousike*,[\[b\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-4) Apollo presides over all music, songs, dance, and poetry. He is the inventor of string-music and the frequent companion of the Muses, functioning as their chorus leader in celebrations. The lyre is a common [attribute of Apollo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#Attributes_and_symbols). Protection of the young is one of the best attested facets of his panhellenic cult persona. As a *[kourotrophos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kourotrophos "Kourotrophos")*, Apollo is concerned with the health and education of children, and he presided over their passage into adulthood. Long hair, which was the prerogative of boys, was cut at the coming of age (*ephebeia*) and dedicated to Apollo. The god himself is depicted with long, uncut hair to symbolise his eternal youth.

Apollo is an important pastoral deity, and he was the patron of herdsmen and shepherds. Protection of herds, flocks and crops from diseases, pests and predators were his primary rustic duties. On the other hand, Apollo also encouraged the founding of new towns and the establishment of civil constitutions, is associated with dominion over [colonists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colonies_in_antiquity "Colonies in antiquity"), and was the giver of laws. His oracles were often consulted before setting laws in a city. Apollo [Agyieus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agyieus "Agyieus") was the protector of the streets, public places and home entrances.[\[3\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-DGRBM-5)

In [Hellenistic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hellenistic "Hellenistic") times, especially during the 5th century BCE, as *Apollo Helios* he became identified among Greeks with [Helios](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Helios "Helios"), the personification of the Sun.[\[4\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-6) Although Latin theological works from at least 1st century BCE identified Apollo with [Sol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sol_\(Roman_mythology\) "Sol (Roman mythology)"),[\[5\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-7)[\[6\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-8) there was no [conflation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conflation "Conflation") between the two among the classical Latin poets until 1st century CE.[\[7\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-9)

## Etymology

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Apollo\&action=edit\&section=1 "Edit section: Etymology")]

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/97/Palestra_grande_di_pompei%2C_affreschi_di_Moregine%2C_terzo_triclinio%2C_IV_stile%2C_epoca_neroniana%2C_05_apollo.jpg/170px-Palestra_grande_di_pompei%2C_affreschi_di_Moregine%2C_terzo_triclinio%2C_IV_stile%2C_epoca_neroniana%2C_05_apollo.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Palestra_grande_di_pompei,_affreschi_di_Moregine,_terzo_triclinio,_IV_stile,_epoca_neroniana,_05_apollo.jpg)

Apollo, fresco from [Pompeii](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pompeii "Pompeii"), 1st century AD

Apollo ([Attic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Attic_Greek "Attic Greek"), [Ionic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ionic_Greek "Ionic Greek"), and [Homeric Greek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Homeric_Greek "Homeric Greek"): Ἀπόλλων, *Apollōn* ([GEN](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Genitive "Genitive") Ἀπόλλωνος); [Doric](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Doric_Greek "Doric Greek"): Ἀπέλλων, *Apellōn*; [Arcadocypriot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arcadocypriot_Greek "Arcadocypriot Greek"): Ἀπείλων, *Apeilōn*; [Aeolic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aeolic_Greek "Aeolic Greek"): Ἄπλουν, *Aploun*; [Latin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Latin_language "Latin language"): *Apollō*)

The name *Apollo*—unlike the related [older name](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Mycenaean_deities "List of Mycenaean deities") *[Paean](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paean_\(god\) "Paean (god)")*—is generally not found in the [Linear B](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Linear_B "Linear B") ([Mycenean Greek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mycenean_Greek "Mycenean Greek")) texts, although there is a possible attestation in the [lacunose](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lacuna_\(manuscripts\) "Lacuna (manuscripts)") form *]pe-rjo-\[* (Linear B: ]𐀟𐁊-\[) on the [KN](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Knossos "Knossos") E 842 tablet,[\[8\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-10)[\[9\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-11)[\[10\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-12) though it has also been suggested that the name might actually read "[Hyperion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hyperion_\(Titan\) "Hyperion (Titan)")" (\[u]-pe-rjo-\[ne]).[\[11\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-13)

The [etymology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Etymology "Etymology") of the name is uncertain. The spelling Ἀπόλλων (pronounced [\[a.pól.lɔːn\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/Greek "Help:IPA/Greek") in [Classical Attic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Attic_Greek "Attic Greek")) had almost superseded all other forms by the beginning of the [common era](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Common_era "Common era"), but the [Doric](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dorians "Dorians") form, *Apellon* (Ἀπέλλων), is more archaic, as it is derived from an earlier \*Ἀπέλjων. It probably is a cognate to the Doric month *Apellaios* (Ἀπελλαῖος),[\[12\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-DDD-14) and the offerings *[apellaia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apellaia "Apellaia")* (ἀπελλαῖα) at the initiation of the young men during the family-festival *apellai* (ἀπέλλαι).[\[13\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-15)[\[14\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-16) According to some scholars, the words are derived from the Doric word *apella* (ἀπέλλα), which originally meant "wall," "fence for animals" and later "assembly within the limits of the square."[\[15\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Nilsson556-17)[\[16\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-18) [Apella](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apella "Apella") (Ἀπέλλα) is the name of the popular assembly in Sparta,[\[15\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Nilsson556-17) corresponding to the *[ecclesia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ecclesia_\(ancient_Athens\) "Ecclesia (ancient Athens)")* (ἐκκλησία). [R. S. P. Beekes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robert_S._P._Beekes "Robert S. P. Beekes") rejected the connection of the theonym with the noun *apellai* and suggested a [Pre-Greek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pre-Greek "Pre-Greek") proto-form \**Apalyun*.[\[17\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-19)

Several instances of [popular etymology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Popular_etymology "Popular etymology") are attested by ancient authors. Thus, the Greeks most often associated Apollo's name with the Greek verb ἀπόλλυμι (*apollymi*), "to destroy".[\[18\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-20) [Plato](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plato "Plato") in *[Cratylus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cratylus "Cratylus")* connects the name with ἀπόλυσις (*apolysis*), "redemption", with ἀπόλουσις (*apolousis*), "purification", and with ἁπλοῦν (*\[h]aploun*), "simple",[\[19\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-21) in particular in reference to the Thessalian form of the name, Ἄπλουν, and finally with Ἀειβάλλων (*aeiballon*), "ever-shooting". [Hesychius](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hesychius_of_Alexandria "Hesychius of Alexandria") connects the name Apollo with the Doric ἀπέλλα (*apella*), which means "assembly", so that Apollo would be the god of political life, and he also gives the explanation σηκός (*sekos*), "fold", in which case Apollo would be the god of flocks and herds.[\[20\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEFreese1911184-22) In the [ancient Macedonian language](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ancient_Macedonian_language "Ancient Macedonian language") πέλλα (*pella*) means "stone,"[\[21\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-23) and some [toponyms](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Toponyms "Toponyms") may be derived from this word: Πέλλα ([Pella](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pella "Pella"),[\[22\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-24) the capital of [ancient Macedonia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Macedonia_\(ancient_kingdom\) "Macedonia (ancient kingdom)")) and Πελλήνη (*Pellēnē*/*[Pellene](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pellene "Pellene")*).[\[23\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-25)

The [Hittite](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hittite_language "Hittite language") form *[Apaliunas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apaliunas "Apaliunas")* (*d**x-ap-pa-li-u-na-aš*) is attested in the [Manapa-Tarhunta letter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manapa-Tarhunta_letter "Manapa-Tarhunta letter").[\[24\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-26) The [Hittite](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hittite_language "Hittite language") testimony reflects an early form *\**Apeljōn**, which may also be surmised from the comparison of Cypriot Ἀπείλων with Doric Ἀπέλλων.[\[25\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-27) The name of the Lydian god *Qλdãns* /kʷʎðãns/ may reflect an earlier /kʷalyán-/ before palatalization, syncope, and the pre-Lydian sound change \*y *>* d.[\[26\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-28) Note the labiovelar in place of the labial /p/ found in pre-Doric Ἀπέλjων and Hittite *Apaliunas*. A [Luwian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luwian_language "Luwian language") etymology suggested for *Apaliunas* makes Apollo "The One of Entrapment", perhaps in the sense of "Hunter".[\[27\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-29)

### Greco-Roman epithets

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Apollo\&action=edit\&section=2 "Edit section: Greco-Roman epithets")]

Apollo's chief [epithet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Epithet "Epithet") was **Phoebus** ([/ˈfiːbəs/](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/English "Help:IPA/English") [*FEE-bəs*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:Pronunciation_respelling_key "Help:Pronunciation respelling key"); Φοῖβος, *Phoibos* Greek pronunciation: [\[pʰó͜i.bos\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/Greek "Help:IPA/Greek")), literally "bright".[\[28\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-30) It was very commonly used by both the Greeks and Romans for Apollo's role as the god of light. Like other Greek deities, he had a number of others applied to him, reflecting the variety of roles, duties, and aspects ascribed to the god. However, while Apollo has a great number of appellations in Greek myth, only a few occur in [Latin literature](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Latin_literature "Latin literature").

#### Sun

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Apollo\&action=edit\&section=3 "Edit section: Sun")]

- **Aegletes** ([/əˈɡliːtiːz/](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/English "Help:IPA/English") [*ə-GLEE-teez*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:Pronunciation_respelling_key "Help:Pronunciation respelling key"); Αἰγλήτης, *Aiglētēs*), from αἴγλη, "light of the Sun"[\[29\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-31)
- **Helius** ([/ˈhiːliəs/](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/English "Help:IPA/English") [*HEE-lee-əs*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:Pronunciation_respelling_key "Help:Pronunciation respelling key"); Ἥλιος, *[Helios](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Helios "Helios")*), literally "Sun"[\[30\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-simbolismo-32)
- **[Lyceus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apollo_Lyceus "Apollo Lyceus")** ([/laɪˈsiːəs/](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/English "Help:IPA/English") [*ly-SEE-əs*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:Pronunciation_respelling_key "Help:Pronunciation respelling key"); Λύκειος, *Lykeios*, from [Proto-Greek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Proto-Greek_language "Proto-Greek language") \*λύκη), "light". The meaning of the epithet "Lyceus" later became associated with Apollo's mother [Leto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leto "Leto"), who was the patron goddess of [Lycia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lycia "Lycia") (Λυκία) and who was identified with the wolf (λύκος).[\[31\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-33)
- **Phanaeus** ([/fəˈniːəs/](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/English "Help:IPA/English") [*fə-NEE-əs*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:Pronunciation_respelling_key "Help:Pronunciation respelling key"); Φαναῖος, *Phanaios*), literally "giving or bringing light"
- **Phoebus** ([/ˈfiːbəs/](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/English "Help:IPA/English") [*FEE-bəs*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:Pronunciation_respelling_key "Help:Pronunciation respelling key"); Φοῖβος, *Phoibos*), literally "bright", his most commonly used epithet by both the Greeks and Romans
- **[Sol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sol_\(Roman_mythology\) "Sol (Roman mythology)")** (Roman) ([/sɒl/](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/English "Help:IPA/English")), "Sun" in Latin

#### Wolf

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Apollo\&action=edit\&section=4 "Edit section: Wolf")]

- **Lycegenes** ([/laɪˈsɛdʒəniːz/](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/English "Help:IPA/English") [*ly-SEJ-ən-eez*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:Pronunciation_respelling_key "Help:Pronunciation respelling key"); Λυκηγενής, *Lukēgenēs*), literally "born of a wolf" or "born of Lycia"
- **Lycoctonus** ([/laɪˈkɒktənəs/](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/English "Help:IPA/English") [*ly-KOK-tə-nəs*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:Pronunciation_respelling_key "Help:Pronunciation respelling key"); Λυκοκτόνος, *Lykoktonos*), from λύκος, "wolf", and κτείνειν, "to kill"

#### Origin and birth

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Apollo\&action=edit\&section=5 "Edit section: Origin and birth")]

Apollo's birthplace was [Mount Cynthus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cynthus "Cynthus") on the island of [Delos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delos "Delos").

- **Cynthius** ([/ˈsɪnθiəs/](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/English "Help:IPA/English") [*SIN-thee-əs*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:Pronunciation_respelling_key "Help:Pronunciation respelling key"); Κύνθιος, *Kunthios*), literally "Cynthian"
- **Cynthogenes** ([/sɪnˈθɒdʒɪniːz/](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/English "Help:IPA/English") [*sin-THOJ-in-eez*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:Pronunciation_respelling_key "Help:Pronunciation respelling key"); Κυνθογενής, *Kynthogenēs*), literally "born of Cynthus"
- **Delius** ([/ˈdiːliəs/](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/English "Help:IPA/English") [*DEE-lee-əs*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:Pronunciation_respelling_key "Help:Pronunciation respelling key"); Δήλιος, *Delios*), literally "Delian"
- **Didymaeus** ([/ˌdɪdɪˈmiːəs/](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/English "Help:IPA/English") [*DID-im-EE-əs*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:Pronunciation_respelling_key "Help:Pronunciation respelling key"); Διδυμαῖος, *Didymaios*) from δίδυμος, "twin", as the twin of [Artemis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Artemis "Artemis")

#### Place of worship

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Apollo\&action=edit\&section=6 "Edit section: Place of worship")]

[Delphi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delphi "Delphi") and [Actium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Actium "Actium") were his primary places of worship.[\[32\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-34)[\[33\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-35)

- **Acraephius** ([/əˈkriːfiəs/](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/English "Help:IPA/English") [*ə-KREE-fee-əs*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:Pronunciation_respelling_key "Help:Pronunciation respelling key"); Ἀκραίφιος, *Akraiphios*, literally "Acraephian") or **Acraephiaeus** ([/əˌkriːfiˈiːəs/](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/English "Help:IPA/English") [*ə-KREE-fee-EE-əs*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:Pronunciation_respelling_key "Help:Pronunciation respelling key"); Ἀκραιφιαίος, *Akraiphiaios*), "Acraephian", from the [Boeotian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boeotia "Boeotia") town of [Acraephia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Acraephia_\(Boeotia\) "Acraephia (Boeotia)") (Ἀκραιφία), reputedly founded by his son [Acraepheus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Acraepheus "Acraepheus").[\[34\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-36)
- **Actiacus** ([/ækˈtaɪəkəs/](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/English "Help:IPA/English") [*ak-TY-ə-kəs*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:Pronunciation_respelling_key "Help:Pronunciation respelling key"); Ἄκτιακός, *Aktiakos*), literally "Actian", after Actium (Ἄκτιον)
- **Delphinius** ([/dɛlˈfɪniəs/](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/English "Help:IPA/English") [*del-FIN-ee-əs*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:Pronunciation_respelling_key "Help:Pronunciation respelling key"); Δελφίνιος, *Delphinios*), literally "Delphic", after Delphi (Δελφοί). An [etiology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Etiology "Etiology") in the *[Homeric Hymns](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Homeric_Hymns "Homeric Hymns")* associated this with dolphins.
- **[Epactaeus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Epactaeus "Epactaeus")**, meaning "god worshipped on the coast", in [Samos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Samos "Samos").[\[35\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-37)
- **Pythius** ([/ˈpɪθiəs/](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/English "Help:IPA/English") [*PITH-ee-əs*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:Pronunciation_respelling_key "Help:Pronunciation respelling key"); Πύθιος, *Puthios*, from Πυθώ, *Pythō*), from the region around Delphi
- **[Smintheus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apollo_Smintheus "Apollo Smintheus")** ([/ˈsmɪnθjuːs/](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/English "Help:IPA/English") [*SMIN-thewss*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:Pronunciation_respelling_key "Help:Pronunciation respelling key"); Σμινθεύς, *Smintheus*), "Sminthian"—that is, "of the town of Sminthos or Sminthe"[\[36\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-LSJsmintheus-38) near the [Troad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Troad "Troad") town of [Hamaxitus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hamaxitus "Hamaxitus")[\[37\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-39)
- **Napaian Apollo** (Ἀπόλλων Ναπαῖος), from the city of [Nape](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nape_\(Lesbos\) "Nape (Lesbos)") at the island of [Lesbos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lesbos "Lesbos")[\[38\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-40)
- **Eutresites**, from the city of [Eutresis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eutresis_\(Boeotia\) "Eutresis (Boeotia)").[\[39\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Harpers_Dictionary_of_Classical_Antiquities-41)

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/74/1993-1994-Giardino_Giusti_%28Verona%29-testo_e_photo_Paolo_Villa-nB08_Cortile-Statua_di_Apollo_-_scultura_Arte_Manierista_-_parete_di_rampicanti_-_Kodak_EktachromeElite_100_5045_EB_100.jpg/220px-thumbnail.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:1993-1994-Giardino_Giusti_\(Verona\)-testo_e_photo_Paolo_Villa-nB08_Cortile-Statua_di_Apollo_-_scultura_Arte_Manierista_-_parete_di_rampicanti_-_Kodak_EktachromeElite_100_5045_EB_100.jpg)

Apollo sculpture, [Palazzo Giusti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palazzo_Giusti "Palazzo Giusti") Verona, [Mannerist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mannerism "Mannerism") art with typical [contrapposto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Contrapposto "Contrapposto")

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9a/William_Birnie_Rhind%2C_Apollo._1889-1894._Glasgow%2C_former_Sun_Life_Building%2C_Renfield_Street._Photo%2C_Jamie_Mulherron.jpg/220px-William_Birnie_Rhind%2C_Apollo._1889-1894._Glasgow%2C_former_Sun_Life_Building%2C_Renfield_Street._Photo%2C_Jamie_Mulherron.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:William_Birnie_Rhind,_Apollo._1889-1894._Glasgow,_former_Sun_Life_Building,_Renfield_Street._Photo,_Jamie_Mulherron.jpg)

William Birnie Rhind, *Apollo* (1889–1894), pediment sculpture, former Sun Life Building, Renfield Street Glasgow

#### Healing and disease

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Apollo\&action=edit\&section=7 "Edit section: Healing and disease")]

- **Acesius** ([/əˈsiːʒəs/](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/English "Help:IPA/English") [*ə-SEE-zhəs*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:Pronunciation_respelling_key "Help:Pronunciation respelling key"); Ἀκέσιος, *Akesios*), from ἄκεσις, "healing". Acesius was the epithet of Apollo worshipped in [Elis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elis_\(city\) "Elis (city)"), where he had a temple in the [agora](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agora "Agora").[\[40\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Smith-42)
- **[Acestor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Acestor "Acestor")** ([/əˈsɛstər/](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/English "Help:IPA/English") [*ə-SESS-tər*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:Pronunciation_respelling_key "Help:Pronunciation respelling key"); Ἀκέστωρ, *Akestōr*), literally "healer"
- **Culicarius** (Roman) ([/ˌkjuːlɪˈkæriəs/](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/English "Help:IPA/English") [*KEW-lih-KARR-ee-əs*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:Pronunciation_respelling_key "Help:Pronunciation respelling key")), from Latin *culicārius*, "of midges"
- **Iatrus** ([/aɪˈætrəs/](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/English "Help:IPA/English") [*eye-AT-rəs*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:Pronunciation_respelling_key "Help:Pronunciation respelling key"); Ἰατρός, *Iātros*), literally "physician"[\[41\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-43)
- **Medicus** (Roman) ([/ˈmɛdɪkəs/](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/English "Help:IPA/English") [*MED-ik-əs*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:Pronunciation_respelling_key "Help:Pronunciation respelling key")), "physician" in Latin. A [temple](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman_temple "Roman temple") was dedicated to *Apollo Medicus* in Rome, probably next to the temple of [Bellona](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bellona_\(goddess\) "Bellona (goddess)").
- **[Paean](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paean_\(god\) "Paean (god)")** ([/ˈpiːən/](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/English "Help:IPA/English") [*PEE-ən*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:Pronunciation_respelling_key "Help:Pronunciation respelling key"); Παιάν, *Paiān*), physician, healer[\[42\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-44)
- **Parnopius** ([/pɑːrˈnoʊpiəs/](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/English "Help:IPA/English") [*par-NOH-pee-əs*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:Pronunciation_respelling_key "Help:Pronunciation respelling key"); Παρνόπιος, *Parnopios*), from πάρνοψ, "locust"

#### Founder and protector

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Apollo\&action=edit\&section=8 "Edit section: Founder and protector")]

- **[Agyieus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agyieus "Agyieus")** ([/əˈdʒaɪɪjuːs/](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/English "Help:IPA/English") [*ə-JUY-ih-yooss*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:Pronunciation_respelling_key "Help:Pronunciation respelling key"); Ἀγυιεύς, *Aguīeus*), from ἄγυια, "street", for his role in protecting roads and homes
- **[Alexicacus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexicacus "Alexicacus")** ([/əˌlɛksɪˈkeɪkəs/](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/English "Help:IPA/English") [*ə-LEK-sih-KAY-kəs*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:Pronunciation_respelling_key "Help:Pronunciation respelling key"); Ἀλεξίκακος, *Alexikakos*), literally "warding off evil"
- **Apotropaeus** ([/əˌpɒtrəˈpiːəs/](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/English "Help:IPA/English") [*ə-POT-rə-PEE-əs*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:Pronunciation_respelling_key "Help:Pronunciation respelling key"); Ἀποτρόπαιος, *Apotropaios*), from ἀποτρέπειν, "to avert"
- **[Archegetes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Archegetes "Archegetes")** ([/ɑːrˈkɛdʒətiːz/](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/English "Help:IPA/English") [*ar-KEJ-ə-teez*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:Pronunciation_respelling_key "Help:Pronunciation respelling key"); Ἀρχηγέτης, *Arkhēgetēs*), literally "founder"
- **Averruncus** (Roman) ([/ˌævəˈrʌŋkəs/](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/English "Help:IPA/English") [*AV-ə-RUNG-kəs*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:Pronunciation_respelling_key "Help:Pronunciation respelling key"); from Latin *āverruncare*), "to avert"
- **Clarius** ([/ˈklæriəs/](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/English "Help:IPA/English") [*KLARR-ee-əs*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:Pronunciation_respelling_key "Help:Pronunciation respelling key"); Κλάριος, *Klārios*), from [Doric](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Doric_Greek "Doric Greek") κλάρος, "allotted lot"[\[43\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-45)
- **Epicurius** ([/ˌɛpɪˈkjʊəriəs/](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/English "Help:IPA/English") [*EP-ih-KURE-ee-əs*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:Pronunciation_respelling_key "Help:Pronunciation respelling key"); Ἐπικούριος, *Epikourios*), from ἐπικουρέειν, "to aid"[\[30\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-simbolismo-32)
- **Genetor** ([/ˈdʒɛnɪtər/](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/English "Help:IPA/English") [*JEN-ih-tər*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:Pronunciation_respelling_key "Help:Pronunciation respelling key"); Γενέτωρ, *Genetōr*), literally "ancestor"[\[30\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-simbolismo-32)
- **Nomius** ([/ˈnoʊmiəs/](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/English "Help:IPA/English") [*NOH-mee-əs*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:Pronunciation_respelling_key "Help:Pronunciation respelling key"); Νόμιος, *Nomios*), literally "pastoral"
- **Nymphegetes** ([/nɪmˈfɛdʒɪtiːz/](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/English "Help:IPA/English") [*nim-FEJ-ih-teez*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:Pronunciation_respelling_key "Help:Pronunciation respelling key"); Νυμφηγέτης, *Numphēgetēs*), from Νύμφη, "Nymph", and ἡγέτης, "leader", for his role as a protector of shepherds and pastoral life
- **Patroos** (Πατρῷος, *Patrōios*) from πατρῷος, "related to one's father," for his role as father of [Ion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ion_\(mythology\) "Ion (mythology)") and founder of the [Ionians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ionians "Ionians"), as worshipped at the [Temple of Apollo Patroos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Temple_of_Apollo_Patroos "Temple of Apollo Patroos") in Athens
- [**Sauroctonus**](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apollo_Sauroctonos "Apollo Sauroctonos") (Σαυροκτόνος, *Sauroctonos*), "lizard-killer", possibly a reference to his killing of [Python](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python_\(mythology\) "Python (mythology)")

#### Prophecy and truth

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Apollo\&action=edit\&section=9 "Edit section: Prophecy and truth")]

- **Coelispex** (Roman) ([/ˈsɛlɪspɛks/](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/English "Help:IPA/English") [*SEL-isp-eks*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:Pronunciation_respelling_key "Help:Pronunciation respelling key")), from Latin *coelum*, "sky", and *specere* "to look at"
- **Iatromantis** ([/aɪˌætrəˈmæntɪs/](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/English "Help:IPA/English") [*eye-AT-rə-MAN-tis*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:Pronunciation_respelling_key "Help:Pronunciation respelling key"); Ἰατρομάντις, *Iātromantis*,) from ἰατρός, "physician", and μάντις, "prophet", referring to his role as a god both of healing and of prophecy
- **Leschenorius** ([/ˌlɛskɪˈnɔːriəs/](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/English "Help:IPA/English") [*LESS-kin-OR-ee-əs*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:Pronunciation_respelling_key "Help:Pronunciation respelling key"); Λεσχηνόριος, *Leskhēnorios*), from λεσχήνωρ, "converser"
- **Loxias** ([/ˈlɒksiəs/](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/English "Help:IPA/English") [*LOK-see-əs*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:Pronunciation_respelling_key "Help:Pronunciation respelling key"); Λοξίας, *Loxias*), from λέγειν, "to say",[\[30\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-simbolismo-32) historically associated with λοξός, "ambiguous"
- **Manticus** ([/ˈmæntɪkəs/](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/English "Help:IPA/English") [*MAN-tik-əs*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:Pronunciation_respelling_key "Help:Pronunciation respelling key"); Μαντικός, *Mantikos*), literally "prophetic"
- **Proopsios** (Προόψιος), meaning "foreseer" or "first seen"[\[44\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-46)

#### Music and arts

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Apollo\&action=edit\&section=10 "Edit section: Music and arts")]

- **Musagetes** ([/mjuːˈsædʒɪtiːz/](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/English "Help:IPA/English") [*mew-SAJ-ih-teez*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:Pronunciation_respelling_key "Help:Pronunciation respelling key"); [Doric](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Doric_Greek "Doric Greek") Μουσαγέτας, *Mousāgetās*), from Μούσα, "[Muse](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muse "Muse")", and ἡγέτης "leader"[\[45\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-47)
- **Musegetes** ([/mjuːˈsɛdʒɪtiːz/](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/English "Help:IPA/English") [*mew-SEJ-ih-teez*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:Pronunciation_respelling_key "Help:Pronunciation respelling key"); Μουσηγέτης, *Mousēgetēs*), as the preceding

#### Archery

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Apollo\&action=edit\&section=11 "Edit section: Archery")]

- **Aphetor** ([/əˈfiːtər/](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/English "Help:IPA/English") [*ə-FEE-tər*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:Pronunciation_respelling_key "Help:Pronunciation respelling key"); Ἀφήτωρ, *Aphētōr*), from ἀφίημι, "to let loose"
- **Aphetorus** ([/əˈfɛtərəs/](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/English "Help:IPA/English") [*ə-FET-ər-əs*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:Pronunciation_respelling_key "Help:Pronunciation respelling key"); Ἀφητόρος, *Aphētoros*), as the preceding
- **Arcitenens** (Roman) ([/ɑːrˈtɪsɪnənz/](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/English "Help:IPA/English") [*ar-TISS-in-ənz*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:Pronunciation_respelling_key "Help:Pronunciation respelling key")), literally "bow-carrying"
- **Argyrotoxus** ([/ˌɑːrdʒərəˈtɒksəs/](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/English "Help:IPA/English") [*AR-jər-ə-TOK-səs*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:Pronunciation_respelling_key "Help:Pronunciation respelling key"); Ἀργυρότοξος, *Argyrotoxos*), literally "with silver bow"
- **Clytotoxus** ([/ˌklaɪtɒˈtɒksəs/](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/English "Help:IPA/English") [*KLY-toh-TOK-səs*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:Pronunciation_respelling_key "Help:Pronunciation respelling key"); Κλυτότοξος, *Klytótoxos*), "he who is famous for his bow", the renowned archer.[\[46\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-48)
- **Hecaërgus** ([/ˌhɛkiˈɜːrɡəs/](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/English "Help:IPA/English") [*HEK-ee-UR-gəs*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:Pronunciation_respelling_key "Help:Pronunciation respelling key"); Ἑκάεργος, *Hekaergos*), literally "far-shooting"
- **Hecebolus** ([/hɪˈsɛbələs/](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/English "Help:IPA/English") [*hiss-EB-əl-əs*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:Pronunciation_respelling_key "Help:Pronunciation respelling key"); Ἑκηβόλος, *Hekēbolos*), "far-shooting"
- **Ismenius** ([/ɪzˈmiːniəs/](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/English "Help:IPA/English") [*iz-MEE-nee-əs*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:Pronunciation_respelling_key "Help:Pronunciation respelling key"); Ἰσμηνιός, *Ismēnios*), literally "of Ismenus", after Ismenus, the son of [Amphion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amphion "Amphion") and [Niobe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Niobe "Niobe"), whom he struck with an arrow

#### Appearance

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Apollo\&action=edit\&section=12 "Edit section: Appearance")]

- **Acersecomes** (Ακερσεκόμης, *Akersekómēs*), "he who has unshorn hair", the eternal ephebe.[\[47\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-49)
- **Chrysocomes** ([/kraɪˈsɒkoʊməs/](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/English "Help:IPA/English") [*cry-SOH-koh-miss*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:Pronunciation_respelling_key "Help:Pronunciation respelling key"); Χρυσοκόμης, *Khrusokómēs*), literally "he who has golden hair."

#### Amazons

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Apollo\&action=edit\&section=13 "Edit section: Amazons")]

- **[Amazonius](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amazonius "Amazonius")** (Ἀμαζόνιος), [Pausanias](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pausanias_\(geographer\) "Pausanias (geographer)") at the [Description of Greece](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Description_of_Greece "Description of Greece") writes that near [Pyrrhichus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pyrrhichus "Pyrrhichus") there was a sanctuary of Apollo, called Amazonius ([Ancient Greek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ancient_Greek_language "Ancient Greek language"): Ἀμαζόνιος) with an image of the god said to have been dedicated by the [Amazons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amazons "Amazons").[\[48\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-50)

#### Other

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Apollo\&action=edit\&section=14 "Edit section: Other")]

- **Boedromius** (Βοηδρόμιος), was a surname of Apollo in Athens, with varying explanations for its origin. Some claim that the reason the god was given this name was because he had helped the Athenians overcome the Amazons in their battle, which took place on the seventh of [Boedromion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boedromion "Boedromion"), the day the [Boedromia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boedromia "Boedromia") were later commemorated. Others claim that the term originated from the fact that, in the battle between Eumolpus and Erechtheus and Ion, Apollo had counselled the Athenians to charge the enemy with a war cry (Βοή) if they were going to win.[\[49\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-51)

### Celtic epithets and cult titles

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Apollo\&action=edit\&section=15 "Edit section: Celtic epithets and cult titles")]

Apollo was worshipped throughout the [Roman Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman_Empire "Roman Empire"). In the traditionally [Celtic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Celtic_nations "Celtic nations") lands, he was most often seen as a healing and sun god. He was often equated with [Celtic gods](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Celtic_gods "List of Celtic gods") of similar character.[\[50\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-52)[\[51\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-53)

- **[Apollo Atepomarus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apollo_Atepomarus "Apollo Atepomarus")** ("the great horseman" or "possessing a great horse"). Apollo was worshipped at [Mauvières](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mauvi%C3%A8res "Mauvières") ([Indre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indre "Indre")). Horses were, in the Celtic world, closely linked to the Sun.[\[52\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-54)
- **[Apollo Belenus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apollo_Belenus "Apollo Belenus")** ("bright" or "brilliant"). This epithet was given to Apollo in parts of [Gaul](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaul "Gaul"), Northern Italy and [Noricum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Noricum "Noricum") (part of modern Austria). Apollo Belenus was a healing and sun god.[\[53\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-55)
- **[Apollo Cunomaglus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apollo_Cunomaglus "Apollo Cunomaglus")** ("hound lord"). A title given to Apollo at a shrine at [Nettleton Shrub](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nettleton_Scrubb "Nettleton Scrubb"), Wiltshire. May have been a god of healing. Cunomaglus himself may originally have been an independent healing god.[\[54\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-56)
- **[Apollo Grannus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apollo_Grannus "Apollo Grannus")**. Grannus was a healing spring god, later equated with Apollo.[\[55\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-57)[\[56\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-thevonat-58)[\[57\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-devries-59)
- **Apollo Maponus**. A god known from inscriptions in Britain. This may be a local fusion of Apollo and [Maponus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maponus "Maponus").
- **[Apollo Moritasgus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apollo_Moritasgus "Apollo Moritasgus")** ("masses of sea water").\[*[clarification needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Please_clarify "Wikipedia:Please clarify")*] An epithet for Apollo at Alesia, where he was worshipped as the god of healing and, possibly, of physicians.[\[58\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-60)
- **[Apollo Vindonnus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apollo_Vindonnus "Apollo Vindonnus")** ("clear light"). Apollo Vindonnus had a temple at [Essarois](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Essarois "Essarois"), near [Châtillon-sur-Seine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ch%C3%A2tillon-sur-Seine "Châtillon-sur-Seine") in present-day [Burgundy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Burgundy "Burgundy"). He was a god of healing, especially of the eyes.[\[56\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-thevonat-58)
- **[Apollo Virotutis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apollo_Virotutis "Apollo Virotutis")** ("benefactor of mankind"). Apollo Virotutis was worshipped, among other places, at Fins d'Annecy ([Haute-Savoie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Haute-Savoie "Haute-Savoie")) and at [Jublains](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jublains "Jublains") ([Maine-et-Loire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maine-et-Loire "Maine-et-Loire")).[\[57\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-devries-59)[\[59\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-61)

## Origins

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Apollo\&action=edit\&section=16 "Edit section: Origins")]

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7a/Omphalos_museum.jpg/250px-Omphalos_museum.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Omphalos_museum.jpg)

[Omphalos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Omphalos "Omphalos") in the Museum of [Delphi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delphi "Delphi")

Apollo is considered the most Hellenic (Greek) of the [Olympian gods](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Olympian_Gods "Olympian Gods").[\[60\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-62)[\[61\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-63)[\[62\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Burkert_1985:143-64)

The cult centers of Apollo in Greece, [Delphi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delphi "Delphi") and [Delos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delos "Delos"), date from the 8th century BCE. The Delos sanctuary was primarily dedicated to [Artemis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Artemis "Artemis"), Apollo's twin sister. At Delphi, Apollo was venerated as the slayer of the monstrous serpent [Python](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python_\(mythology\) "Python (mythology)"). For the Greeks, Apollo was the most Greek of all the gods, and through the centuries he acquired different functions. In [Archaic Greece](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Archaic_Greece "Archaic Greece") he was the [prophet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prophet "Prophet"), the oracular god who in older times was connected with "healing". In [Classical Greece](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Classical_Greece "Classical Greece") he was the god of light and of music, but in popular religion he had a strong function to keep away evil.[\[63\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-65) [Walter Burkert](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Walter_Burkert "Walter Burkert") discerned three components in the prehistory of Apollo worship, which he termed "a Dorian-northwest Greek component, a Cretan-Minoan component, and a Syro-Hittite component."[\[64\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-66)

### Healer and god-protector from evil

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Apollo\&action=edit\&section=17 "Edit section: Healer and god-protector from evil")]

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3a/Apollo-WaltersArt.jpg/200px-Apollo-WaltersArt.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Apollo-WaltersArt.jpg)

*Apollo Victorious over the Python* by [Pietro Francavilla](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pietro_Francavilla "Pietro Francavilla") (1591), depicting Apollo's victory over the serpent Python[\[65\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-67) ([The Walters Art Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Walters_Art_Museum "Walters Art Museum"))

In classical times, his major function in popular religion was to keep away evil, and he was therefore called "apotropaios" (ἀποτρόπαιος, "averting evil") and "alexikakos" (ἀλεξίκακος "keeping off ill"; from [v.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Verb "Verb") ἀλέξω + [n.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Noun "Noun") κακόν).[\[66\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-68) Apollo also had many epithets relating to his function as a healer. Some commonly-used examples are "paion" (παιών literally "healer" or "helper")[\[67\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-69) "epikourios" (ἐπικούριος, "succouring"), "oulios" (οὔλιος, "healer, baleful")[\[68\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-70) and "loimios" (λοίμιος, "of the plague"). In later writers, the word, "paion", usually spelled "Paean", becomes a mere epithet of Apollo in his capacity as a god of [healing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Healing "Healing").[\[69\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-71)

Apollo in his aspect of "healer" has a connection to the primitive god [Paean](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paean_\(god\) "Paean (god)") (Παιών-Παιήων), who did not have a cult of his own. Paean serves as the healer of the gods in the *[Iliad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iliad "Iliad")*, and seems to have originated in a pre-Greek religion.[\[70\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-72) It is suggested, though unconfirmed, that he is connected to the [Mycenaean](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mycenaean_Greece "Mycenaean Greece") figure *pa-ja-wo-ne* (Linear B: 𐀞𐀊𐀺𐀚).[\[71\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-73)[\[72\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-74)[\[73\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Chawick-75) Paean was the personification of holy songs sung by "seer-doctors" (ἰατρομάντεις), which were supposed to cure disease.[\[74\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-76)

Homer uses the noun Paeon to designate both a god and that god's characteristic song of [apotropaic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apotropaic "Apotropaic") thanksgiving and triumph.[\[75\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-77) Such songs were originally addressed to Apollo and afterwards to other gods: to [Dionysus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dionysus "Dionysus"), to Apollo [Helios](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Helios "Helios"), to Apollo's son [Asclepius](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asclepius "Asclepius") the healer. About the 4th century BCE, the paean became merely a formula of adulation; its object was either to implore protection against disease and misfortune or to offer thanks after such protection had been rendered. It was in this way that Apollo had become recognized as the god of music. Apollo's role as the slayer of the [Python](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python_\(mythology\) "Python (mythology)") led to his association with battle and victory; hence it became the [Roman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ancient_Rome "Ancient Rome") custom for a paean to be sung by an army on the march and before entering into battle, when a fleet left the harbour, and also after a victory had been won.\[*[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed "Wikipedia:Citation needed")*]

In the *Iliad*, Apollo is the healer under the gods, but he is also the bringer of disease and death with his arrows, similar to the function of the [Vedic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vedic "Vedic") god of disease [Rudra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rudra "Rudra").[\[76\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Martin_Nilsson_1967_p._541-78) He sends a plague (λοιμός) to the [Achaeans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Achaeans_\(Homer\) "Achaeans (Homer)"). Knowing that Apollo can prevent a recurrence of the plague he sent, they purify themselves in a ritual and offer him a large sacrifice of cows, called a [hecatomb](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hecatomb "Hecatomb").[\[77\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-79)

### Dorian origin

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Apollo\&action=edit\&section=18 "Edit section: Dorian origin")]

The *[Homeric Hymn to Apollo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Homeric_Hymns "Homeric Hymns")* depicts Apollo as an intruder from the north.[\[78\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-80) The connection with the northern-dwelling [Dorians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dorians "Dorians") and their initiation festival *[apellai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apellai "Apellai")* is reinforced by the month *Apellaios* in northwest Greek calendars.[\[79\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-81) The family-festival was dedicated to Apollo ([Doric](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dorians "Dorians"): Ἀπέλλων).[\[80\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-82) *Apellaios* is the month of these rites, and Apellon is the "megistos kouros" (the great Kouros).[\[81\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-83) However it can explain only the Doric type of the name, which is connected with the [Ancient Macedonian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ancient_Macedonian_language "Ancient Macedonian language") word "pella" ([Pella](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pella "Pella")), *stone*. Stones played an important part in the cult of the god, especially in the oracular shrine of Delphi ([Omphalos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Omphalos "Omphalos")).[\[82\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-84)[\[83\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-85)

### Minoan origin

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Apollo\&action=edit\&section=19 "Edit section: Minoan origin")]

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c0/AMI_-_Goldene_Doppelaxt.jpg/250px-AMI_-_Goldene_Doppelaxt.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:AMI_-_Goldene_Doppelaxt.jpg)

Ornamented golden [Minoan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minoan_civilization "Minoan civilization") [labrys](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Labrys "Labrys")

George Huxley considered the identification of Apollo with the Minoan deity Paiawon, worshipped in Crete, to have originated at Delphi.[\[84\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-86) In the *Homeric Hymn*, Apollo appears as a dolphin carrying Cretan priests to Delphi, to which site they evidently transfer their religious practices. *Apollo Delphinios* or *Delphidios* was a sea-god worshipped especially in Crete and in the islands.[\[85\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-87) Apollo's sister [Artemis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Artemis "Artemis"), who was the Greek goddess of hunting, is identified with the Minoan goddess [Britomartis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Britomartis "Britomartis") (Diktynna), and with *Laphria* the Pre-Greek "mistress of the animals" who was specially worshipped at Delphi. In her earliest depictions she was accompanied by the "Master of the animals", a bow-wielding god of hunting whose name has been lost; aspects of this figure may have been absorbed into the more popular Apollo.[\[86\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Nilsson499-88) A family of priests at Delphi was named "Lab(r)yaden". The name may derive from Laphria.[\[87\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-89)

### Anatolian origin

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Apollo\&action=edit\&section=20 "Edit section: Anatolian origin")]

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/6/6b/Coin_of_Apollo_Agyieus.png)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Coin_of_Apollo_Agyieus.png)

Illustration of a coin of Apollo Agyieus from [Ambracia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ambracia "Ambracia")

A non-Greek origin of Apollo has long been assumed in scholarship.[\[12\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-DDD-14) The name of Apollo's mother [Leto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leto "Leto") has [Lydian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lydia "Lydia") origin, and she was worshipped on the coasts of [Asia Minor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asia_Minor "Asia Minor"). The inspiration oracular cult was probably introduced into Greece from [Anatolia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anatolia "Anatolia"), which is the origin of [Sibyl](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sibyl "Sibyl"), and where some of the oldest oracular shrines originated. Omens, symbols, purifications, and exorcisms appear in old [Assyro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Assyria "Assyria")-[Babylonian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Babylon "Babylon") texts. These rituals were spread into the empire of the [Hittites](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hittites "Hittites"), and from there into Greece.[\[88\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Nilsson563-90)

[Homer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Homer "Homer") pictures Apollo on the side of the [Trojans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Troy "Troy"), fighting against the [Achaeans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Achaeans_\(Homer\) "Achaeans (Homer)"), during the [Trojan War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trojan_War "Trojan War"). He is pictured as a terrible god, less trusted by the Greeks than other gods. The god seems to be related to *Appaliunas*, a tutelary god of [Wilusa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wilusa "Wilusa") ([Troy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Troy "Troy")) in Asia Minor, but the word is not complete.[\[89\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-91) The stones found in front of the gates of [Homeric](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Homer "Homer") Troy were the symbols of Apollo. A western Anatolian origin may also be bolstered by references to the parallel worship of *Artimus* ([Artemis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Artemis "Artemis")) and *Qλdãns*, whose name may be cognate with the Hittite and Doric forms, in surviving [Lydian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lydian_language "Lydian language") text&#x73;*.*[\[90\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-92) However, recent scholars have cast doubt on the identification of *Qλdãns* with Apollo.[\[91\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-93)

The Greeks gave to him the name ἀγυιεύς *[agyieus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agyieus "Agyieus")* as the protector god of public places and houses who wards off evil and his symbol was a tapered stone or column.[\[92\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-94) However, while usually Greek festivals were celebrated at the [full moon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Full_moon "Full moon"), all the feasts of Apollo were celebrated on the seventh day of the month, and the emphasis given to that day (*sibutu*) indicates a [Babylonian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Babylonia "Babylonia") origin.[\[93\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-95)

### Proto-Indo-European

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Apollo\&action=edit\&section=21 "Edit section: Proto-Indo-European")]

The [Vedic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vedic "Vedic") [Rudra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rudra "Rudra") has some functions similar to those of Apollo. The terrible god is called "the archer" and the bow is also an attribute of [Shiva](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shiva "Shiva").[\[94\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-96) Rudra could bring diseases with his arrows, but he was able to free people of them and his alternative Shiva is a healer physician god.[\[95\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-97) However the [Indo-European](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indo-European_language "Indo-European language") component of Apollo does not explain his strong association with omens, exorcisms, and an oracular cult.\[*[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed "Wikipedia:Citation needed")*]

## Oracular cult

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Apollo\&action=edit\&section=22 "Edit section: Oracular cult")]

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f0/Columns_of_the_Temple_of_Apollo_at_Delphi%2C_Greece.jpeg/250px-Columns_of_the_Temple_of_Apollo_at_Delphi%2C_Greece.jpeg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Columns_of_the_Temple_of_Apollo_at_Delphi,_Greece.jpeg)

Columns of the [Temple of Apollo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Temple_of_Apollo_\(Delphi\) "Temple of Apollo (Delphi)") at Delphi, Greece

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9a/Herakles_tripod_Louvre_F341.jpg/250px-Herakles_tripod_Louvre_F341.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Herakles_tripod_Louvre_F341.jpg)

Oracular tripod

Unusually among the Olympic deities, Apollo had two cult sites that had widespread influence: [Delos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delos "Delos") and [Delphi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delphi "Delphi"). In cult practice, [Delian Apollo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delos "Delos") and [Pythian Apollo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pythian_Apollo "Pythian Apollo") (the Apollo of Delphi) were so distinct that they might both have shrines in the same locality.[\[62\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Burkert_1985:143-64) [Lycia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lycia "Lycia") was sacred to the god, for this Apollo was also called Lycian.[\[96\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-98)[\[97\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-99) Apollo's [cult](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cult_\(religion\) "Cult (religion)") was already fully established when written sources commenced, about 650 BCE. Apollo became extremely important to the Greek world as an oracular deity in the [archaic period](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Archaic_Greece "Archaic Greece"), and the frequency of [theophoric names](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Theophoric_names "Theophoric names") such as *Apollodorus* or *Apollonios* and cities named *Apollonia* testify to his popularity. Oracular sanctuaries to Apollo were established in other sites. In the 2nd and 3rd century CE, those at [Didyma](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Didyma "Didyma") and [Claros](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Claros "Claros") pronounced the so-called "theological oracles", in which Apollo confirms that all deities are aspects or servants of an [all-encompassing, highest deity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monism#Monism,_pantheism,_and_panentheism "Monism"). "In the 3rd century, Apollo fell silent. [Julian the Apostate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Julian_the_Apostate "Julian the Apostate") (359–361) tried to revive the Delphic oracle, but failed."[\[12\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-DDD-14)

### Oracular shrines

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Apollo\&action=edit\&section=23 "Edit section: Oracular shrines")]

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a5/Delos_lions.jpg/250px-Delos_lions.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Delos_lions.jpg)

Delos lions

Apollo had a famous [oracle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oracle "Oracle") in Delphi, and other notable ones in [Claros](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Claros "Claros") and [Didyma](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Didyma "Didyma"). His oracular shrine in [Abae](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abae "Abae") in [Phocis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Phocis "Phocis"), where he bore the [toponymic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Toponym "Toponym") epithet *[Abaeus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abaeus "Abaeus")* (Ἀπόλλων Ἀβαῖος, *Apollon Abaios*), was important enough to be consulted by [Croesus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Croesus "Croesus").[\[98\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-100) His oracular shrines include:

- [Abae](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abae "Abae") in [Phocis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Phocis "Phocis").
- [Bassae](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bassae "Bassae") in the [Peloponnese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peloponnese "Peloponnese").
- At [Clarus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clarus "Clarus"), on the west coast of [Asia Minor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asia_Minor "Asia Minor"); as at Delphi a holy spring which gave off a *pneuma*, from which the priests drank.
- In [Corinth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ancient_Corinth "Ancient Corinth"), the Oracle of Corinth came from the town of [Tenea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tenea "Tenea"), from prisoners supposedly taken in the Trojan War.
- At [Khyrse](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Khyrse\&action=edit\&redlink=1 "Khyrse (page does not exist)"), in [Troad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Troad "Troad"), the temple was built for Apollo Smintheus.
- In [Delos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delos "Delos"), there was an oracle to the Delian Apollo, during summer. The Hieron (Sanctuary) of Apollo adjacent to the Sacred Lake, was the place where the god was said to have been born.
- In [Delphi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delphi "Delphi"), the [Pythia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pythia "Pythia") became filled with the *[pneuma](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pneuma "Pneuma")* of Apollo, said to come from a spring inside the [Adyton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adyton "Adyton").
- In [Didyma](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Didyma "Didyma"), an oracle on the coast of [Anatolia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anatolia "Anatolia"), south west of [Lydian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lydia "Lydia") ([Luwian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luwian "Luwian")) [Sardis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sardis "Sardis"), in which priests from the lineage of the Branchidae received inspiration by drinking from a healing spring located in the temple. Was believed to have been founded by [Branchus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Branchus "Branchus"), son or lover of Apollo.
- In [Hierapolis Bambyce](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manbij "Manbij"), Syria (modern Manbij), according to the treatise *[De Dea Syria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/De_Dea_Syria "De Dea Syria")*, the sanctuary of the [Syrian Goddess](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atargatis "Atargatis") contained a robed and bearded image of Apollo. Divination was based on spontaneous movements of this image.[\[99\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-101)
- At [Patara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Patara_\(Lycia\) "Patara (Lycia)"), in [Lycia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lycia "Lycia"), there was a seasonal winter oracle of Apollo, said to have been the place where the god went from Delos. As at Delphi the oracle at Patara was a woman.
- In [Segesta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Segesta "Segesta") in Sicily.

Oracles were also given by sons of Apollo.

- In [Oropus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oropus "Oropus"), north of [Athens](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Athens "Athens"), the oracle [Amphiaraus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amphiaraus "Amphiaraus"), was said to be the son of Apollo; Oropus also had a sacred spring.
- in Labadea, 20 miles (32 km) east of Delphi, [Trophonius](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trophonius "Trophonius"), another son of Apollo, killed his brother and fled to the cave where he was also afterwards consulted as an oracle.

## Temples of Apollo

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Apollo\&action=edit\&section=24 "Edit section: Temples of Apollo")]

Main articles: [Ancient Greek temple](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ancient_Greek_temple "Ancient Greek temple") and [Roman temple](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman_temple "Roman temple")

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/0c/The_Temple_of_Apollo_Epikourios_at_Bassae%2C_east_colonnade%2C_Arcadia%2C_Greece_%2814087181020%29.jpg/250px-The_Temple_of_Apollo_Epikourios_at_Bassae%2C_east_colonnade%2C_Arcadia%2C_Greece_%2814087181020%29.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:The_Temple_of_Apollo_Epikourios_at_Bassae,_east_colonnade,_Arcadia,_Greece_\(14087181020\).jpg)

Partial view of the temple of Apollo Epikurios (healer) at [Bassae](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bassae "Bassae") in southern Greece

Many temples were dedicated to Apollo in Greece and the Greek colonies. They show the spread of the cult of Apollo and the evolution of Greek architecture, which was mostly based on the rightness of form and on mathematical relations. Some of the earliest temples, especially in [Crete](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crete "Crete"), do not belong to any Greek order. It seems that the first peripteral temples were rectangular wooden structures. The different wooden elements were considered [divine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Divinity "Divinity"), and their forms were preserved in the marble or stone elements of the temples of [Doric order](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Doric_order "Doric order"). The Greeks used standard types because they believed that the world of objects was a series of typical forms which could be represented in several instances. The temples should be [canonic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canon_\(basic_principle\) "Canon (basic principle)"), and the architects were trying to achieve this esthetic perfection.[\[100\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-102) From the earliest times there were certain rules strictly observed in rectangular peripteral and prostyle buildings. The first buildings were built narrowly in order to hold the roof, and when the dimensions changed some mathematical relations became necessary in order to keep the original forms. This probably influenced the theory of numbers of [Pythagoras](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pythagoras "Pythagoras"), who believed that behind the appearance of things there was the permanent principle of mathematics.[\[101\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-C._M._Bowra_1957_p._166-103)

The [Doric order](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Doric_order "Doric order") dominated during the 6th and the 5th century BC but there was a mathematical problem regarding the position of the triglyphs, which could not be solved without changing the original forms. The order was almost abandoned for the [Ionic order](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ionic_order "Ionic order"), but the Ionic capital also posed an insoluble problem at the corner of a temple. Both orders were abandoned for the [Corinthian order](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Corinthian_order "Corinthian order") gradually during the Hellenistic age and under Rome.\[*[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed "Wikipedia:Citation needed")*]

The most important temples are:

### Greek temples

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Apollo\&action=edit\&section=25 "Edit section: Greek temples")]

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d3/TempleDelos.jpg/250px-TempleDelos.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:TempleDelos.jpg)

Temple of the Delians at [Delos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delos "Delos"), dedicated to Apollo (478 BC). 19th-century pen-and-wash restoration.

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/70/Chryse.jpg/250px-Chryse.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Chryse.jpg)

Temple of Apollo Smintheus in [Çanakkale Province](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C3%87anakkale_Province "Çanakkale Province"), Turkey

- [Thebes, Greece](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thebes,_Greece "Thebes, Greece"): The oldest temple probably dedicated to *Apollo Ismenius* was built in the 9th century BC. It seems that it was a curvilinear building. The [Doric](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Doric_order "Doric order") temple was built in the early 7th century BC, but only some small parts have been found.[\[102\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-104) A festival called [Daphnephoria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daphnephoria "Daphnephoria") was celebrated every ninth year in honour of Apollo Ismenius (or Galaxius). The people held laurel branches (daphnai), and at the head of the procession walked a youth (chosen priest of Apollo), who was called "daphnephoros".[\[103\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-William_Smith_1875._p._384-105)
- [Eretria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eretria "Eretria"): According to the Homeric hymn to Apollo, the god arrived on the plain, seeking for a location to establish its oracle. The first temple of *Apollo Daphnephoros*, "Apollo, laurel-bearer", or "carrying off Daphne", is dated to 800 BC. The temple was curvilinear *hecatombedon* (a hundred feet). In a smaller building were kept the bases of the laurel branches which were used for the first building. Another temple probably peripteral was built in the 7th century BC, with an inner row of wooden columns over its Geometric predecessor. It was rebuilt peripteral around 510 BC, with the [stylobate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stylobate "Stylobate") measuring 21.00 x 43.00 m. The number of [pteron](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pteron "Pteron") column was 6 x 14.[\[104\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-106)[\[105\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-107)
- [Dreros](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dreros "Dreros") ([Crete](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crete "Crete")). The temple of *Apollo Delphinios* dates from the 7th century BC, or probably from the middle of the 8th century BC. According to the legend, Apollo appeared as a dolphin, and carried Cretan priests to the port of [Delphi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delphi "Delphi").[\[106\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Martin_Nilsson_1967_p._529-108) The dimensions of the plan are 10.70 x 24.00 m and the building was not peripteral. It contains column-bases of the [Minoan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minoan_civilization "Minoan civilization") type, which may be considered as the predecessors of the [Doric](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Doric_order "Doric order") columns.[\[107\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-109)
- [Gortyn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gortyn "Gortyn") ([Crete](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crete "Crete")). A temple of *Pythian Apollo*, was built in the 7th century BC. The plan measured 19.00 x 16.70 m and it was not peripteral. The walls were solid, made from limestone, and there was a single door on the east side.\[*[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed "Wikipedia:Citation needed")*]
- [Thermon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thermon "Thermon") ([West Greece](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/West_Greece "West Greece")): The [Doric](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Doric_order "Doric order") temple of *Apollo Thermios*, was built in the middle of the 7th century BC. It was built on an older curvilinear building dating perhaps from the 10th century, on which a [peristyle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peristyle "Peristyle") was added. The temple was narrow, and the number of pteron columns (probably wooden) was 5 x 15. There was a single row of inner columns. It measures 12.13 x 38.23 m at the stylobate, which was made from stones.[\[108\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Spivey,_p._112-110)

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/29/Apollotempel.gif/200px-Apollotempel.gif)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Apollotempel.gif)

Floor plan of the temple of Apollo, Corinth

- [Corinth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Corinth "Corinth"): A [Doric](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Doric_order "Doric order") temple was built in the 6th century BC. The temple's [stylobate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stylobate "Stylobate") measures 21.36 x 53.30 m, and the number of pteron columns was 6 x 15. There was a double row of inner columns. The style is similar to the Temple of Alcmeonidae at [Delphi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delphi "Delphi").[\[109\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-111) The Corinthians were considered to be the inventors of the [Doric order](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Doric_order "Doric order").[\[108\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Spivey,_p._112-110)
- Napes ([Lesbos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lesbos "Lesbos")): An [Aeolic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aeolic_order "Aeolic order") temple probably of *Apollo Napaios* was built in the 7th century BC. Some special capitals with floral ornament have been found, which are called [Aeolic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aeolic_order "Aeolic order"), and it seems that they were borrowed from the East.[\[110\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Robertson324-112)
- [Cyrene, Libya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cyrene,_Libya "Cyrene, Libya"): The oldest [Doric](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Doric_order "Doric order") temple of Apollo was built in c. 600 BC. The number of pteron columns was 6 x 11, and it measures 16.75 x 30.05 m at the stylobate. There was a double row of sixteen inner columns on stylobates. The capitals were made from stone.[\[110\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Robertson324-112)

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/34/Apollonaukratis.png/220px-Apollonaukratis.png)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Apollonaukratis.png)

Inscriptions for Apollo, Naukratis

- [Naukratis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Naukratis "Naukratis"): An [Ionic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ionic_order "Ionic order") temple was built in the early 6th century BC. Only some fragments have been found and the earlier ones, made from limestone, are identified among the oldest of the [Ionic order](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ionic_order "Ionic order").[\[111\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-113)

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e5/Siracusa-Temple-of-Apoll-Plan-bjs.png/200px-Siracusa-Temple-of-Apoll-Plan-bjs.png)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Siracusa-Temple-of-Apoll-Plan-bjs.png)

Floor plan of the temple of Apollo, Syracuse

- [Syracuse, Sicily](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syracuse,_Sicily "Syracuse, Sicily"): A [Doric](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Doric_order "Doric order") temple was built at the beginning of the 6th century BC. The temple's [stylobate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stylobate "Stylobate") measures 21.47 x 55.36 m and the number of pteron columns was 6 x 17. It was the first temple in Greek west built completely out of stone. A second row of columns were added, obtaining the effect of an inner porch.[\[112\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-114)
- [Selinus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Selinus "Selinus") ([Sicily](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sicily "Sicily")):The [Doric](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Doric_order "Doric order") [Temple C](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Temple_C_\(Selinus\) "Temple C (Selinus)") dates from 550 BC, and it was probably dedicated to Apollo. The temple's stylobate measures 10.48 x 41.63 m and the number of pteron columns was 6 x 17. There was a portico with a second row of columns, which is also attested for the temple at [Syracuse](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syracuse,_Sicily "Syracuse, Sicily").[\[113\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-115)
- [Delphi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delphi "Delphi"): The first temple dedicated to Apollo, was built in the 7th century BC. According to the legend, it was wooden made of laurel branches. The "Temple of Alcmeonidae" was built in c. 513 BC and it is the oldest Doric temple with significant marble elements. The temple's stylobate measures 21.65 x 58.00 m, and the number of pteron columns as 6 x 15.[\[114\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-116) A fest similar with Apollo's fest at [Thebes, Greece](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thebes,_Greece "Thebes, Greece") was celebrated every nine years. A boy was sent to the temple, who walked on the sacred road and returned carrying a laurel branch (*dopnephoros*). The maidens participated with joyful songs.[\[103\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-William_Smith_1875._p._384-105)
- [Chios](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chios "Chios"): An [Ionic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ionic_order "Ionic order") temple of *Apollo Phanaios* was built at the end of the 6th century BC. Only some small parts have been found and the capitals had floral ornament.[\[110\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Robertson324-112)
- [Abae](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abae "Abae") ([Phocis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Phocis_\(ancient_region\) "Phocis (ancient region)")). The temple was destroyed by the [Persians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Persia "Persia") in the invasion of [Xerxes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Xerxes_I_of_Persia "Xerxes I of Persia") in 480 BC, and later by the [Boeotians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boeotia "Boeotia"). It was rebuilt by [Hadrian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hadrian "Hadrian").[\[115\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-117) The oracle was in use from early [Mycenaean](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mycenae "Mycenae") times to the Roman period, and shows the continuity of Mycenaean and Classical Greek religion.[\[116\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-118)

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1f/Bassai_Temple_of_Apollo_Plan.svg/200px-Bassai_Temple_of_Apollo_Plan.svg.png)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Bassai_Temple_of_Apollo_Plan.svg)

Floor plan of the Temple of Apollo at Bassae

- [Bassae](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bassae "Bassae") ([Peloponnesus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peloponnesus "Peloponnesus")): A temple dedicated to *Apollo Epikourios* ("Apollo the helper"), was built in 430 BC, designed by [Iktinos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iktinos "Iktinos"). It combined [Doric](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Doric_order "Doric order") and [Ionic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ionic_order "Ionic order") elements, and the earliest use of a column with a [Corinthian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Corinthian_order "Corinthian order") capital in the middle.[\[117\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-119) The temple is of a relatively modest size, with the [stylobate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stylobate "Stylobate") measuring 14.5 x 38.3 metres containing a [Doric](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Doric_order "Doric order") [peristyle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peristyle "Peristyle") of 6 x 15 columns. The roof left a central space open to admit light and air.[\[118\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-WHO-120)
- [Delos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delos "Delos"): A temple probably dedicated to Apollo and not peripteral, was built in the late 7th century BC, with a plan measuring 10.00 x 15.60 m. The [Doric](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Doric_order "Doric order") Great temple of Apollo, was built in c. 475 BC. The temple's stylobate measures 13.72 x 29.78 m, and the number of pteron columns as 6 x 13. Marble was extensively used.[\[110\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Robertson324-112)
- [Ambracia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ambracia "Ambracia"): A [Doric](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Doric_order "Doric order") peripteral temple dedicated to *Apollo Pythios Sotir* was built in 500 BC, at the centre of the Greek city [Arta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arta,_Greece "Arta, Greece"). Only some parts have been found, and it seems that the temple was built on earlier sanctuaries dedicated to Apollo. The temple measures 20.75 x 44.00 m at the [stylobate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stylobate "Stylobate"). The foundation which supported the statue of the god, still exists.[\[119\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-121)
