[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d2/Tempel_des_Apollo_Didymaeos_in_Milet.png/180px-Tempel_des_Apollo_Didymaeos_in_Milet.png)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Tempel_des_Apollo_Didymaeos_in_Milet.png)

Temple of Apollo, Didyma

- [Didyma](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Didyma "Didyma") (near [Miletus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Miletus "Miletus")): The gigantic [Ionic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ionic_order "Ionic order") temple of *Apollo Didymaios* started around 540 BC. The construction ceased and then it was restarted in 330 BC. The temple is dipteral, with an outer row of 10 x 21 columns, and it measures 28.90 x 80.75 m at the stylobate.[\[120\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-122)
- [Clarus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clarus "Clarus") (near ancient [Colophon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colophon_\(city\) "Colophon (city)")): According to the legend, the famous seer Calchas, on his return from Troy, came to Clarus. He challenged the seer Mopsus, and died when he lost.[\[121\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-123) The [Doric](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Doric_order "Doric order") temple of *Apollo Clarius* was probably built in the 3rd century BC., and it was peripteral with 6 x 11 columns. It was reconstructed at the end of the Hellenistic period, and later from the emperor [Hadrian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hadrian "Hadrian") but Pausanias claims that it was still incomplete in the 2nd century BC.[\[122\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-124)
- [Hamaxitus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hamaxitus "Hamaxitus") ([Troad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Troad "Troad")): In the [Iliad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iliad "Iliad"), [Chryses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chryses "Chryses") the priest of Apollo, addresses the god with the epithet Smintheus (Lord of Mice), related to the god's ancient role as bringer of the disease (plague). Recent excavations indicate that the Hellenistic temple of *Apollo Smintheus* was constructed in 150–125 BC, but the symbol of the mouse god was used on coinage probably from the 4th century .[\[123\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-125) The temple measures 40.00 x 23.00 m at the [stylobate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stylobate "Stylobate"), and the number of pteron columns was 8 x 14.[\[124\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-126)
- Pythion ([Ancient Greek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ancient_Greek_language "Ancient Greek language"): Πύθιον), this was the name of a shrine of Apollo at [Athens](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Athens "Athens") near the [Ilisos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ilisos "Ilisos") river. It was created by Peisistratos, and tripods were placed there by those who had won in the cyclic chorus at the [Thargelia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thargelia "Thargelia").[\[125\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-127)
- [Setae (Lydia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Setae_\(Lydia\) "Setae (Lydia)"): The temple of *Apollo Aksyros* located in the city.[\[126\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-128)
- [Apollonia Pontica](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apollonia_Pontica "Apollonia Pontica"): There were two temples of Apollo Healer in the city. One from the Late Archaic period and the other from the Early Classical period.[\[127\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-129)
- [Ikaros island in the Persian Gulf](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ikaros_\(Failaka_Island\) "Ikaros (Failaka Island)") (modern [Failaka Island](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Failaka_Island "Failaka Island")): There was a temple of Apollo on the island.[\[128\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-130)
- Argos in [Cyprus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cyprus "Cyprus"): there was a temple of Apollo Erithios (Ἐριθίου Ἀπόλλωνος ἱερῷ).[\[129\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-131)
- The temple and oracle of Apollo at [Eutresis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eutresis_\(Boeotia\) "Eutresis (Boeotia)").[\[39\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Harpers_Dictionary_of_Classical_Antiquities-41)
- An altar of Apollo Acritas was at [Lacedaemon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lacedaemon "Lacedaemon"). In addition, above a sanctuary surnamed Gasepton of Earth in Lacedaemon was set up the Maleatian Apollo.[\[130\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-132)

### Etruscan and Roman temples

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Apollo\&action=edit\&section=26 "Edit section: Etruscan and Roman temples")]

- [Veii](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Veii "Veii") ([Etruria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Etruria "Etruria")): The temple of Apollo was built in the late 6th century BC, indicating the spread of Apollo's culture (Aplu) in Etruria. There was a prostyle porch, which is called [Tuscan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tuscan_order "Tuscan order"), and a triple cella 18.50 m wide.[\[131\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Robertson_pp._200-201-133)
- [Falerii Veteres](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Falerii_Veteres "Falerii Veteres") ([Etruria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Etruria "Etruria")): A temple of Apollo was built probably in the 4th–3rd century BC. Parts of a terracotta capital, and a terracotta base have been found. It seems that the Etruscan columns were derived from the archaic Doric.[\[131\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Robertson_pp._200-201-133) A cult of [Apollo Soranus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soranus_\(mythology\) "Soranus (mythology)") is attested by one inscription found near Falerii.[\[132\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-134)

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/30/Pompeii_Regio_07_Insula_07_Temple_of_Apollo_plan_01.jpg/180px-Pompeii_Regio_07_Insula_07_Temple_of_Apollo_plan_01.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Pompeii_Regio_07_Insula_07_Temple_of_Apollo_plan_01.jpg)

Plan of the [Temple of Apollo (Pompeii)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Temple_of_Apollo_\(Pompeii\) "Temple of Apollo (Pompeii)")

- [Pompeii](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pompeii "Pompeii") (Italy): The cult of Apollo was widespread in the region of Campania since the 6th century BC. The temple was built in 120 BC, but its beginnings lie in the 6th century BC. It was reconstructed after an earthquake in AD 63. It demonstrates a mixing of styles which formed the basis of Roman architecture. The columns in front of the cella formed a [Tuscan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tuscan_order "Tuscan order") prostyle porch, and the cella is situated unusually far back. The peripteral colonnade of 48 [Ionic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ionic_order "Ionic order") columns was placed in such a way that the emphasis was given to the front side.[\[133\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-135)
- Rome: The [temple of Apollo Sosianus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Temple_of_Apollo_Sosianus "Temple of Apollo Sosianus") and the *temple of Apollo Medicus*. The first temple building dates to 431 BC, and was dedicated to Apollo Medicus (the doctor), after a plague of 433 BC.[\[134\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-136) It was rebuilt by [Gaius Sosius](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaius_Sosius "Gaius Sosius"), probably in 34 BC. Only three columns with [Corinthian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Corinthian_order "Corinthian order") capitals exist today. It seems that the cult of Apollo had existed in this area since at least to the mid-5th century BC.[\[135\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-137)
- Rome: The [temple of Apollo Palatinus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Temple_of_Apollo_Palatinus "Temple of Apollo Palatinus") was located on the Palatine hill within the sacred boundary of the city. It was dedicated by [Augustus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Augustus "Augustus") in 28 BC. The façade of the original temple was [Ionic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ionic_order "Ionic order") and it was constructed from solid blocks of marble. Many famous statues by Greek masters were on display in and around the temple, including a marble statue of the god at the entrance and a statue of Apollo in the cella.[\[136\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-138)
- [Melite](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Melite_\(ancient_city\) "Melite (ancient city)") (modern [Mdina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mdina "Mdina"), [Malta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malta "Malta")): A [Temple of Apollo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Temple_of_Apollo_\(Melite\) "Temple of Apollo (Melite)") was built in the city in the 2nd century AD. Its remains were discovered in the 18th century, and many of its architectural fragments were dispersed among private collections or reworked into new sculptures. Parts of the temple's podium were rediscovered in 2002.[\[137\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-139)

## Mythology

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Apollo\&action=edit\&section=27 "Edit section: Mythology")]

Main article: [Greek mythology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greek_mythology "Greek mythology")

In the myths, Apollo is the son of [Zeus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zeus "Zeus"), the king of the gods, and [Leto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leto "Leto"), his previous wife[\[138\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-140) or one of his mistresses. Apollo often appears in the myths, plays and hymns either directly or indirectly through his oracles. As Zeus' favorite son, he had direct access to the mind of Zeus and was willing to reveal this knowledge to humans. A divinity beyond human comprehension, he appears both as a beneficial and a wrathful god.\[*[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed "Wikipedia:Citation needed")*]

### Birth

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Apollo\&action=edit\&section=28 "Edit section: Birth")]

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/19/Metropolitan_Richart_Latona.jpg/220px-Metropolitan_Richart_Latona.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Metropolitan_Richart_Latona.jpg)

Leto with her children, by [William Henry Rinehart](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/William_Henry_Rinehart "William Henry Rinehart")

#### Homeric Hymn to Apollo

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Apollo\&action=edit\&section=29 "Edit section: Homeric Hymn to Apollo")]

Pregnant with the offsprings of Zeus, Leto wandered through many lands wanting to give birth to Apollo. However all the lands rejected her out of fear. Upon reaching Delos, Leto requested the island to shelter her, and that in return her son would bring fame and prosperity to the island. Delos then revealed to Leto that Apollo was rumoured to be the god who will "greatly lord it among gods and men all over the fruitful earth". For this reason, all the lands were fearful and Delos feared that Apollo would cast her aside once he is born. Hearing this, Leto swore on the river Styx that if she is allowed to give birth on the island, her son would honour Delos the most amongst all the other lands. Assured by this, Delos agreed to assist Leto. All goddesses except Hera also came to aid Leto.[\[139\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-:0-141)

However, Hera had tricked [Eileithyia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eileithyia "Eileithyia"), the goddess of childbirth, to stay on Olympus, due to which Leto was unable to give birth. The goddesses then convinced Iris to go bring Eileithyia by offering her a necklace of amber 9 yards (8.2 m) long. Iris did accordingly and persuaded Eileithyia to step onto the island. Thus, clutching a palm tree, Leto finally gave birth after labouring for nine days and nine nights, with Apollo "leaping forth" from his mother's womb. The goddesses washed the newborn, covered him in a white garment and fastened golden bands around him. As Leto was unable to feed him, [Themis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Themis "Themis"), the goddess of divine law, fed him nectar and [ambrosia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ambrosia "Ambrosia"). Upon tasting the divine food, the child broke free of the bands fastened onto him and declared that he would be the master of [lyre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lyre "Lyre") and archery, and interpret the will of Zeus to humankind. He then started to walk, which caused the island to be filled with gold.[\[139\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-:0-141)

#### Callimachus' hymn to Delos

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Apollo\&action=edit\&section=30 "Edit section: Callimachus' hymn to Delos")]

The island Delos used to be [Asteria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asteria "Asteria"), a goddess who jumped into the waters to escape the advances of Zeus and became a free-floating island of the same name. When Leto got pregnant, Hera was told that Leto's son would become more dear to Zeus than Ares. Enraged by this, Hera watched over the heavens and sent out Ares and Iris to prevent Leto from giving birth on the earth. Ares, stationed over the mainland, and Iris, over the islands, threatened all the lands and prevented them from helping Leto.[\[140\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-:1-142)

When Leto arrived at Thebes, fetal Apollo prophesied from his mother's womb that in the future he would punish a slanderous woman in Thebes ([Niobe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Niobe "Niobe")), so he did not want to be born there. Leto then went to Thessaly and sought the help of the river nymphs who were the daughters of the river Peneus. Though he was initially fearful and reluctant, Peneus later decided to let Leto give birth in his waters. He did not change his mind even when Ares produced a terrifying sound and threatened to hurl mountain peaks into the river. But Leto herself declined his help and departed, as she did not want him to suffer for her sake.[\[140\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-:1-142)

After being turned away from various lands, Apollo spoke again from the womb, asking his mother to take look at the floating island in front of her and expressing his wish to be born there. When Leto approached Asteria, all the other islands fled. But Asteria welcomed Leto without any fear of Hera. Walking on the island, she sat down against a palm tree and asked Apollo to be born. During the childbirth, swans circled the island seven times, a sign that later on Apollo would play the seven-stringed lyre. When Apollo finally "leapt forth" from his mother's womb, the nymphs of the island sang a hymn to Eileithyia that was heard to the heavens. The moment Apollo was born, the entire island, including the trees and the waters, became gold. Asteria bathed the newborn, swaddled him and fed him with her breast milk. The island had become rooted and was later called Delos.[\[140\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-:1-142)

Hera was no longer angry, as Zeus had managed to calm her down; and she held no grudge against Asteria, since Asteria had rejected Zeus in the past.[\[140\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-:1-142)

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/06/Latona_with_Her_Children_Apollo_and_Diana_LACMA_M.78.86.jpg/220px-Latona_with_Her_Children_Apollo_and_Diana_LACMA_M.78.86.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Latona_with_Her_Children_Apollo_and_Diana_LACMA_M.78.86.jpg)

Leto holding Apollo, by Lazar Widmann

#### Pindar's fragments

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Apollo\&action=edit\&section=31 "Edit section: Pindar's fragments")]

Pindar is the earliest source who explicitly calls Apollo and Artemis as twins. Here, Asteria is also stated to be Leto's sister. Wanting to escape Zeus' advances, she flung herself into the sea and became a floating rock called Ortygia until the twins were born.[\[141\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-143) When Leto stepped on the rock, four pillars with adamantine bases rose from the earth and held up the rock.[\[142\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-144) When Apollo and Artemis were born, their bodies shone radiantly and a chant was sung by Eileithyia and [Lachesis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lachesis "Lachesis"), one of the three [Moirai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moirai "Moirai").[\[143\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-145)

#### Pseudo-Hyginus

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Apollo\&action=edit\&section=32 "Edit section: Pseudo-Hyginus")]

Scorning the advances of Zeus, Asteria transformed herself into a bird and jumped into a sea. From her, an island rose which was called Ortygia.[\[144\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-146) When Hera discovered that Leto was pregnant with Zeus' child, she decreed that Leto can give birth only in a place where sun does not shine. During this time, the monster Python also started hounding Leto with an intent of killing her, because he had foreseen his death coming at the hands of Leto's offspring. However, on Zeus' orders, [Boreas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boreas_\(god\) "Boreas (god)") carried away Leto and entrusted her to [Poseidon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Poseidon "Poseidon"). To protect her, Poseidon took her to the island Ortygia and covered it with waves so that the sun would not shine on it. Leto gave birth clinging to an olive tree and henceforth the island was called Delos.[\[145\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-147)

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1f/Latona_with_the_infants_Apollo_and_Artemis%2C_by_Francesco_Pozzi%2C_1824%2C_marble_-_Sculpture_Gallery%2C_Chatsworth_House_-_Derbyshire%2C_England_-_DSC03504.jpg/220px-Latona_with_the_infants_Apollo_and_Artemis%2C_by_Francesco_Pozzi%2C_1824%2C_marble_-_Sculpture_Gallery%2C_Chatsworth_House_-_Derbyshire%2C_England_-_DSC03504.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Latona_with_the_infants_Apollo_and_Artemis,_by_Francesco_Pozzi,_1824,_marble_-_Sculpture_Gallery,_Chatsworth_House_-_Derbyshire,_England_-_DSC03504.jpg)

Leto with Apollo and Artemis, by [Francesco Pozzi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Francesco_Pozzi "Francesco Pozzi")

#### Other variations

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Apollo\&action=edit\&section=33 "Edit section: Other variations")]

Aside from those mentioned above, more variations on the story of Apollo's birth include:

Aelian states that it took Leto twelve days and twelve nights to travel from Hyperborea to Delos.[\[146\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-148) Leto changed herself into a she-wolf before giving birth. This is given as the reason why Homer describes Apollo as the "wolf-born god".[\[147\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-149)[\[148\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-150)

Libanius wrote that neither land nor visible islands would receive Leto, but by the will of Zeus Delos then became visible, and thus received Leto and the children.[\[149\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-151)

According to Strabo, the [Curetes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Curetes_\(tribe\) "Curetes (tribe)") helped Leto by creating loud noises with their weapons and thus frightening Hera, they concealed Leto's childbirth.[\[150\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-152)

Theognis wrote that the island was filled with ambrosial fragrance when Apollo was born, and the Earth laughed with joy.[\[151\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-153)

In some versions, [Artemis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Artemis "Artemis") was born first and subsequently assisted with the birth of Apollo.[\[152\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-154)[\[153\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-155)

While in some accounts Apollo's birth itself fixed the floating Delos to the earth, there are accounts of Apollo securing Delos to the bottom of the ocean a little while later.[\[154\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-156)[\[155\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-157) This island became sacred to Apollo and was one of the major cult centres of the god. Apollo was born on the seventh day (ἑβδομαγενής, *hebdomagenes*)[\[156\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-158) of the month [Thargelion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Attic_calendar#Festival_calendar_\(lunisolar\) "Attic calendar")—according to Delian tradition—or of the month [Bysios](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hellenic_calendars#Delphic "Hellenic calendars")—according to Delphian tradition. The seventh and twentieth, the days of the new and full moon, were ever afterwards held sacred to him.[\[20\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEFreese1911184-22)

### Hyperborea

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Apollo\&action=edit\&section=34 "Edit section: Hyperborea")]

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e5/Antalya_Museum_06022022_009.jpg/220px-Antalya_Museum_06022022_009.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Antalya_Museum_06022022_009.jpg)

Apollo head in the [Antalya Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antalya_Museum "Antalya Museum"), [Turkey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkey "Turkey")

[Hyperborea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hyperborea "Hyperborea"), the mystical land of eternal spring, venerated Apollo above all the gods. The Hyperboreans always sang and danced in his honor and hosted [Pythian games](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pythian_games "Pythian games").[\[157\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-159) There, a vast forest of beautiful trees was called "the garden of Apollo". Apollo spent the winter months among the Hyperboreans,[\[158\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-160)[\[159\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-ReferenceA-161) leaving his shrine in Delphi under the care of Dionysus. His absence from the world caused coldness and this was marked as his annual death. No prophecies were issued during this time.[\[160\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-ReferenceC-162) He returned to the world during the beginning of the spring. The *Theophania* festival was held in [Delphi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delphi "Delphi") to celebrate his return.[\[161\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-163)

However, Diodorus Silculus states that Apollo visited Hyperborea every nineteen years. This nineteen-year period was called by the Greeks as the 'year of Meton', the time period in which the stars returned to their initial positions. And that visiting Hyperborea at that time, Apollo played on the cithara and danced continuously from the vernal equinox until the rising of the [Pleiades](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pleiades_\(Greek_mythology\) "Pleiades (Greek mythology)") (constellations).[\[162\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-164)

Hyperborea was also Leto's birthplace. It is said that Leto came to Delos from Hyperborea accompanied by a pack of wolves. Henceforth, Hyperborea became Apollo's winter home and wolves became sacred to him. His intimate connection to wolves is evident from his epithet *Lyceus*, meaning *wolf-like*. But Apollo was also the wolf-slayer in his role as the god who protected flocks from predators. The Hyperborean worship of Apollo bears the strongest marks of Apollo being worshipped as the sun god. Shamanistic elements in Apollo's cult are often liked to his Hyperborean origin, and he is likewise speculated to have originated as a solar shaman.[\[163\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-165)[\[164\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-166) Shamans like [Abaris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abaris "Abaris") and [Aristeas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aristeas "Aristeas") were also the followers of Apollo, who hailed from Hyperborea.\[*[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed "Wikipedia:Citation needed")*]

In myths, the tears of amber Apollo shed when his son Asclepius died mixed with the waters of the river Eridanos, which surrounded Hyperborea. Apollo also buried in Hyperborea the arrow which he had used to kill the [Cyclopes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cyclopes "Cyclopes"). He later gave this arrow to Abaris.[\[165\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-167)

### Childhood and youth

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Apollo\&action=edit\&section=35 "Edit section: Childhood and youth")]

Growing up, Apollo was nursed by the nymphs Korythalia and [Aletheia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aletheia_\(disambiguation\) "Aletheia (disambiguation)"), the personification of truth.[\[166\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-168) [Phoebe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Phoebe_\(Titaness\) "Phoebe (Titaness)"), his grandmother, gave the oracular shrine of [Delphi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delphi "Delphi") to Apollo as a birthday gift.[\[167\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-ReferenceG-169)

As a four-year-old child, Apollo built a foundation and an altar on Delos using the horns of the goats that his sister Artemis hunted. Since he learnt the art of building when young, he came to be known as [Archegetes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Archegetes "Archegetes"), (*the founder of towns*) and guided men to build new cities.[\[168\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-170) To keep the child amused, the Delian nymphs ran around the altar beating it, and then with their hands tied behind their backs, bit an olive branch. It later became a custom for all the sailors who passed by the island to do the same.[\[169\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Callimachus,_Hymn_to_Delos-171)

From his father Zeus, Apollo received a golden headband and a chariot driven by swans.[\[170\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Alcaeus,_Hymn_to_Apollo-172)[\[171\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-ReferenceF-173)

In his early years when Apollo spent his time herding cows, he was reared by the [Thriae](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thriae "Thriae"), who trained him and enhanced his prophetic skills.[\[172\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-174) The god Pan was also said to have mentored him in the prophetic art.[\[173\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-175) Apollo is also said to have invented the lyre, and along with Artemis, the art of archery. He then taught the humans the art of healing and archery.[\[174\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-176)

#### Lycian peasants

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Apollo\&action=edit\&section=36 "Edit section: Lycian peasants")]

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/fa/Joshua_Cristall_-_Latona_and_the_Lycian_Peasants_-_96.1210_-_Museum_of_Fine_Arts.jpg/220px-Joshua_Cristall_-_Latona_and_the_Lycian_Peasants_-_96.1210_-_Museum_of_Fine_Arts.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Joshua_Cristall_-_Latona_and_the_Lycian_Peasants_-_96.1210_-_Museum_of_Fine_Arts.jpg)

Latona and the Lycian peasants, by [Joshua Cristall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joshua_Cristall "Joshua Cristall")

Soon after giving birth to her twins, Leto fled from Delos fearing Hera. Upon reaching Lycia, her infants had drained all of their mother's milk and cried for more to satisfy their hunger. The exhausted mother then tried drinking from a nearby lake but was stopped by some [Lycian peasants](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lycian_peasants "Lycian peasants"). When she begged them to let her quench her thirst, the haughty peasants not only threatened her but also stirred the mud in the lake to dirty the waters. Angered by this, Leto turned them into frogs.[\[175\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-177)

In a slightly varied version, Leto took her infants and crossed over to Lycia where she attempted to bathe her children in a spring she found there. But the local herdsmen drove her away. After that, some wolves found Leto and guided her to the river Xanthos, where Leto was able to bathe her children and quench her thirst. She then returned to the spring and turned the herdsmen into frogs.[\[176\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-178)

#### Slaying of Python

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Apollo\&action=edit\&section=37 "Edit section: Slaying of Python")]

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b6/7003.Apollo_mit_dem_get%C3%B6teten_Python%281752%29-Fran%C3%A7ois_Gaspard_Adam-Gro%C3%9Fe_Font%C3%A4ne-Sanssouci_Steffen_Heilfort.JPG/190px-7003.Apollo_mit_dem_get%C3%B6teten_Python%281752%29-Fran%C3%A7ois_Gaspard_Adam-Gro%C3%9Fe_Font%C3%A4ne-Sanssouci_Steffen_Heilfort.JPG)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:7003.Apollo_mit_dem_get%C3%B6teten_Python\(1752\)-Fran%C3%A7ois_Gaspard_Adam-Gro%C3%9Fe_Font%C3%A4ne-Sanssouci_Steffen_Heilfort.JPG)

Apollo victorious over the Python, by [François Gaspard Adam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fran%C3%A7ois_Gaspard_Adam "François Gaspard Adam")

[Python](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python_\(mythology\) "Python (mythology)"), a [chthonic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chthonic "Chthonic") serpent-dragon, was a child of Gaia and the guardian of the [Delphic Oracle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delphic_Oracle "Delphic Oracle"). In the Callimachus' hymn to Delos, fetal Apollo foresees the death of Python at his hands.[\[169\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Callimachus,_Hymn_to_Delos-171)

In the Homeric hymn to Apollo, Python was a female drakon and the nurse of the giant [Typhon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Typhon "Typhon") whom Hera had created to overthrow Zeus. She was described as a terrifying monster and a "bloody plague". Apollo, in his pursuit to establish his worship, came across Python and killed her with a single arrow shot from his bow. He let the corpse rot under the sun and declared himself the oracular deity of Delphi.[\[177\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Homeric_hymn_to_Apollo-179) Other authors have Apollo kill the monster using a hundred arrows[\[178\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-180)[\[179\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-181) or a thousand arrows.[\[180\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Ovid,_Metamorphoses_1._434-182)

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d8/Artus_Quellinus%2C_Apollo_en_Python-_Apollon_et_Python%2C_KBS-FRB.jpg/220px-Artus_Quellinus%2C_Apollo_en_Python-_Apollon_et_Python%2C_KBS-FRB.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Artus_Quellinus,_Apollo_en_Python-_Apollon_et_Python,_KBS-FRB.jpg)

*Apollo and Python*, terracotta relief by [Artus Quellinus the Elder](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Artus_Quellinus_the_Elder "Artus Quellinus the Elder") (1609–1668)

According to Euripides, Leto had brought her twins to the cliffs of Parnassus shortly after giving birth to them. Upon seeing the monster there, Apollo, still a child being carried in his mother's arms, leapt forth and killed Python.[\[181\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-183) Some authors also mention that Python was killed for displaying lustful affections towards Leto.[\[182\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-184)[\[183\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-185)

In another account, Python chased pregnant Leto with an intent of killing her because his death was fated to come at the hands of Leto's child. However, he had to stop the chase when Leto came under the protection of Poseidon. After his birth, four days old Apollo killed the serpent with the bow and arrows gifted to him by Hephaestus and avenged the trouble given to his mother. The god then put the bones of the slain monster in a cauldron and deposited it in his temple.[\[184\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Pseudo-Hyginus,_Fabulae_140-186)

This legend is also narrated as the origin of the cry "*Hië paian*". According to Athenaeus, Python attacked Leto and her twins during their visit to Delphi. Taking Artemis into her arms, Leto climbed upon a rock and cried at Apollo to shoot the monster. The cry let out by her, "ιε, παῖ" ("Shoot, boy") later got slightly altered as "ἰὴ παιών" (*Hië paian*), an exclamation to avert evils.[\[185\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-187) Callimachus attributes the origin of this phrase to the Delphians, who let out the cry to encourage Apollo when the young god battled with Python.[\[186\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-188)

Strabo has recorded a slightly different version where Python was actually a cruel and lawless man who was also known by the name "Drakon". When Apollo was teaching the humans to cultivate fruits and civilise themselves, the residents of Parnassus complained to the god about Python. In response to their pleas, Apollo killed the man with his arrows. During the fight, the Parnassians shouted *"Hië paian"* to encourage the god.[\[187\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Strabo,_Geography_9._3._12-189)

#### Establishment of worship in Delphi

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Apollo\&action=edit\&section=38 "Edit section: Establishment of worship in Delphi")]

Continuing from his victory over Python, the Homeric hymn describes how the young god established his worship among the humans. As Apollo was pondering about what kind of men he should recruit to serve him, he spotted a ship full of Cretan merchants or pirates. He took the form of a dolphin and sprang aboard the ship. Whenever the oblivious crew members tried throwing the dolphin overboard, the god shook the ship until the crew was awed into submission. Apollo then created a breeze that directed the ship to Delphi. Upon reaching the land, he revealed himself as a god and initiated them as his priests. He instructed them to guard his temple and always keep righteousness in their hearts.[\[188\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-190)

Alcaeus narrates the following account: Zeus, who had adorned his newborn son with a golden headband, also provided him with a chariot driven by swans and instructed Apollo to visit Delphi to establish his laws among the people. But Apollo disobeyed his father and went to the land of [Hyperborea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hyperborea "Hyperborea"). The Delphians continuously sung paeans in his honour and pleaded him to come back to them. The god returned only after a year and then carried out Zeus' orders.[\[170\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Alcaeus,_Hymn_to_Apollo-172)[\[189\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-191)

In other variations, the shrine at Delphi was simply handed over to Apollo by his grandmother Phoebe as a gift,[\[167\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-ReferenceG-169) or Themis herself inspired him to be the oracular voice of Delphi.[\[190\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-192)

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/bd/John_Flaxman_-_To_Phoebus_at_His_Birth%2C_From_Aeschylus%2C_Furies_-_Google_Art_Project.jpg/220px-John_Flaxman_-_To_Phoebus_at_His_Birth%2C_From_Aeschylus%2C_Furies_-_Google_Art_Project.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:John_Flaxman_-_To_Phoebus_at_His_Birth,_From_Aeschylus,_Furies_-_Google_Art_Project.jpg)

[Phoebe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Phoebe_\(Titaness\) "Phoebe (Titaness)") gifts the oracular tripod to Apollo, by [John Flaxman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Flaxman "John Flaxman")

However, in many other accounts, Apollo had to overcome certain obstacles before he was able to establish himself at Delphi. Gaea came in conflict with Apollo for killing Python and claiming the Delphic oracle for himself. According to Pindar, she sought to banish Apollo to Tartarus as a punishment.[\[191\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-193)[\[192\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-194) According to Euripides, soon after Apollo took the ownership of the oracle, Gaea started sending prophetic dreams to the humans. As a result, people stopped visiting Delphi to obtain prophecies. Troubled by this, Apollo went to Olympus and supplicated to Zeus. Zeus, admiring the ambitions of his young son, granted his request by putting an end to the dream visions. This sealed the role of Apollo as the oracular deity of Delphi.[\[193\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-195)

Since Apollo had committed a blood crime, he also had to be purified. Pausanias has recorded two of the many variations of this purification. In one of them, both Apollo and Artemis fled to Sicyon and were purified there.[\[194\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-196) In the other tradition that had been prevalent among the Cretans, Apollo alone travelled to Crete and was purified by [Carmanor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carmanor_\(of_Crete\) "Carmanor (of Crete)").[\[195\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-197) In another account, the Argive king [Crotopus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crotopus "Crotopus") was the one who performed the purification rites on Apollo alone.[\[196\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-198)

According the Aristonous and Aelian, Apollo was purified by the will of Zeus in the [Vale of Tempe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vale_of_Tempe "Vale of Tempe").[\[197\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-199) Aristonous has continued the tale, saying that Apollo was escorted back to Delphi by Athena. As a token of gratitude, he later built a temple for Athena at Delphi, which served as a threshold for his own temple.[\[198\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-200) Upon reaching Delphi, Apollo convinced Gaea and Themis into handing over the seat of oracle to him. To celebrate this event, other immortals also graced Apollo with gifts – Poseidon gave him the land of Delphi, the Delphian nymphs gifted him the Corycian cave, and Artemis set her dogs to patrol and safeguard the land.[\[199\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-201)

Some others have also said that Apollo was exiled and subjected to servitude under king [Admetus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Admetus "Admetus") as a means of punishment for the murder he had committed.[\[200\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-202) It was when he was serving as a cowherd under Admetus that the theft of the cattle by Hermes happened.[\[201\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-203)[\[202\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-204) The servitude was said to have lasted for either one year,[\[203\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-205)[\[204\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-206) or one great year (a cycle of eight years),[\[205\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-207)[\[206\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-208) or nine years.[\[207\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-209)

Plutarch, however, has mentioned a variation where Apollo was neither purified in Tempe nor banished to Earth as a servant for nine years, but was driven out to another world for nine great years. The god who returned was cleansed and purified, thus becoming a "true Phoebus – that is to say, clear and bright". He then took over the Delphic oracle, which had been under the care of Themis in his absence.[\[208\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-210) Henceforth, Apollo became the god who cleansed himself from the sin of murder, made men aware of their guilt and purified them.[\[209\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-211)

The Pythian games were also established by Apollo, either as funeral games to honor Python[\[184\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Pseudo-Hyginus,_Fabulae_140-186)[\[210\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-ReferenceE-212) or to celebrate his own victory.[\[211\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-213)[\[212\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-214)[\[180\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Ovid,_Metamorphoses_1._434-182) The [Pythia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pythia "Pythia") was Apollo's high priestess and his mouthpiece through whom he gave prophecies.[\[213\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-215)

#### Tityus

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Apollo\&action=edit\&section=39 "Edit section: Tityus")]

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/14/Apollo_Tityos_Staatliche_Antikensammlungen_2689.jpg/220px-Apollo_Tityos_Staatliche_Antikensammlungen_2689.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Apollo_Tityos_Staatliche_Antikensammlungen_2689.jpg)

Apollo slaying [Tityos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tityos "Tityos"), Attic red-figure kylix, 460–450 BC

[Tityus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tityos "Tityos") was another giant who tried to rape Leto, either on his own accord when she was on her way to Delphi[\[214\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Homer,_Odyssey_11._576-216)[\[215\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Apollodorus,_Bibliotheca_1.22-217) or at the order of Hera.[\[216\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-218) Leto called upon on her children who instantly slew the giant. Apollo, still a young boy, shot him with his arrows.[\[217\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-219)[\[218\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-220) In some accounts, Artemis also joined him in protecting their mother by attacking Tityos with her arrows.[\[219\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-221)[\[220\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-222) For this act, he was banished to Tartarus and there he was pegged to the rock floor and stretched on an area of 9 acres (36,000 m2), while a pair of vultures feasted daily on his liver[\[214\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Homer,_Odyssey_11._576-216) or his heart.[\[215\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Apollodorus,_Bibliotheca_1.22-217)

Another account recorded by Strabo says that Tityus was not a giant but a lawless man whom Apollo killed at the request of the residents.[\[187\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Strabo,_Geography_9._3._12-189)

### Admetus

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Apollo\&action=edit\&section=40 "Edit section: Admetus")]

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/24/Drawing%2C_Apollo_Guards_the_Herds_%28or_Flocks%29_of_King_Admetus%2C_1780%E2%80%931800_%28CH_18122047%29.jpg/220px-Drawing%2C_Apollo_Guards_the_Herds_%28or_Flocks%29_of_King_Admetus%2C_1780%E2%80%931800_%28CH_18122047%29.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Drawing,_Apollo_Guards_the_Herds_\(or_Flocks\)_of_King_Admetus,_1780%E2%80%931800_\(CH_18122047\).jpg)

Apollo guards the herds (or flocks) of King [Admetus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Admetus "Admetus"), by [Felice Gianni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Felice_Giani "Felice Giani")

[Admetus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Admetus "Admetus") was the king of [Pherae](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pherae "Pherae"), who was known for his hospitality. When Apollo was exiled from Olympus for killing Python, he served as a herdsman under Admetus, who was then young and unmarried. Apollo is said to have shared a romantic relationship with Admetus during his stay.[\[159\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-ReferenceA-161) After completing his years of servitude, Apollo went back to Olympus as a god.\[*[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed "Wikipedia:Citation needed")*]

Because Admetus had treated Apollo well, the god conferred great benefits on him in return. Apollo's mere presence is said to have made the cattle give birth to twins.[\[221\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-bib-3-10-4-223)[\[159\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-ReferenceA-161) Apollo helped Admetus win the hand of [Alcestis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alcestis "Alcestis"), the daughter of [King Pelias](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pelias "Pelias"),[\[222\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-bib-1-9-15-224)[\[223\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-hyginus-50-225) by taming a lion and a boar to draw Admetus' chariot. He was present during their wedding to give his blessings. When Admetus angered the goddess Artemis by forgetting to give her the due offerings, Apollo came to the rescue and calmed his sister.[\[222\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-bib-1-9-15-224) When Apollo learnt of Admetus' untimely death, he convinced or tricked the [Fates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moirai "Moirai") into letting Admetus live past his time.[\[222\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-bib-1-9-15-224)[\[223\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-hyginus-50-225)

According to another version, or perhaps some years later, when Zeus struck down Apollo's son [Asclepius](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asclepius "Asclepius") with a lightning bolt for resurrecting the dead, Apollo in revenge killed the [Cyclopes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cyclopes "Cyclopes"), who had fashioned the bolt for Zeus.[\[221\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-bib-3-10-4-223) Apollo would have been banished to [Tartarus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tartarus "Tartarus") for this, but his mother [Leto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leto "Leto") intervened, and reminding Zeus of their old love, pleaded with him not to kill their son. Zeus obliged and sentenced Apollo to one year of [hard labor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Penal_labour "Penal labour") once again under Admetus.[\[221\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-bib-3-10-4-223)

The love between Apollo and Admetus was a favored topic of Roman poets like [Ovid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ovid "Ovid") and [Servius](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maurus_Servius_Honoratus "Maurus Servius Honoratus").\[*[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed "Wikipedia:Citation needed")*]

### Niobe

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Apollo\&action=edit\&section=41 "Edit section: Niobe")]

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d8/Pierre-Charles_Jombert_-_Les_enfants_de_Niob%C3%A9_tu%C3%A9s_par_Apollon_et_Diane.JPG/220px-Pierre-Charles_Jombert_-_Les_enfants_de_Niob%C3%A9_tu%C3%A9s_par_Apollon_et_Diane.JPG)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Pierre-Charles_Jombert_-_Les_enfants_de_Niob%C3%A9_tu%C3%A9s_par_Apollon_et_Diane.JPG)

Niobe's children are killed by Apollo and Diana, by [Pierre-Charles Jombert](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pierre-Charles_Jombert "Pierre-Charles Jombert")

The fate of [Niobe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Niobe "Niobe") was prophesied by Apollo while he was still in Leto's womb.[\[159\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-ReferenceA-161) Niobe was the queen of [Thebes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thebes_\(Greece\) "Thebes (Greece)") and wife of [Amphion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amphion "Amphion"). She displayed [hubris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hubris "Hubris") when she boasted that she was superior to Leto because she had fourteen children ([Niobids](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Niobids "Niobids")), seven male and seven female, while Leto had only two. She further mocked Apollo's effeminate appearance and Artemis' manly appearance. Leto, insulted by this, told her children to punish Niobe. Accordingly, Apollo killed Niobe's sons, and Artemis her daughters. According to some versions of the myth, among the Niobids, Chloris and her brother Amyclas were not killed because they prayed to Leto. Amphion, at the sight of his dead sons, either killed himself or was killed by Apollo after swearing revenge.\[*[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed "Wikipedia:Citation needed")*]

A devastated Niobe fled to [Mount Sipylos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spil_Mount "Spil Mount") in [Asia Minor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asia_Minor "Asia Minor") and turned into stone as she wept. Her tears formed the river [Achelous](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Achelous "Achelous"). Zeus had turned all the people of Thebes to stone and so no one buried the Niobids until the ninth day after their death, when the gods themselves entombed them.\[*[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed "Wikipedia:Citation needed")*]

When Chloris married and had children, Apollo granted her son [Nestor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nestor_\(mythology\) "Nestor (mythology)") the years he had taken away from the Niobids. Hence, Nestor was able to live for 3 generations.[\[224\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-226)

### Building the walls of Troy

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Apollo\&action=edit\&section=42 "Edit section: Building the walls of Troy")]

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/ff/Sandrart_%28attributed%29%2C_Troppa_%28attr.%29_-_Laomedon_Refusing_Payment_to_Poseidon_and_Apollo_-_17th_c.jpg/220px-Sandrart_%28attributed%29%2C_Troppa_%28attr.%29_-_Laomedon_Refusing_Payment_to_Poseidon_and_Apollo_-_17th_c.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Sandrart_\(attributed\),_Troppa_\(attr.\)_-_Laomedon_Refusing_Payment_to_Poseidon_and_Apollo_-_17th_c.jpg)

Laomedon refusing payment to Poseidon and Apollo, by [Joachim von Sandrart](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joachim_von_Sandrart "Joachim von Sandrart")

Once Apollo and [Poseidon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Poseidon "Poseidon") served under the Trojan king [Laomedon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Laomedon "Laomedon") in accordance with Zeus' words. Apollodorus states that the gods willingly went to the king disguised as humans in order to check his hubris.[\[225\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-227) Apollo guarded the cattle of Laomedon in the valleys of Mount Ida, while Poseidon built the walls of Troy.[\[226\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-228) Other versions make both Apollo and Poseidon the builders of the wall. In Ovid's account, Apollo completes his task by playing his tunes on his lyre.\[*[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed "Wikipedia:Citation needed")*]

In [Pindar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pindar "Pindar")'s odes, the gods took a mortal named [Aeacus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aeacus "Aeacus") as their assistant.[\[227\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-229) When the work was completed, three snakes rushed against the wall, and though the two that attacked the sections of the wall built by the gods fell down dead, the third forced its way into the city through the portion of the wall built by Aeacus. Apollo immediately prophesied that [Troy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Troy "Troy") would fall at the hands of Aeacus's descendants, the [Aeacidae](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aeacidae "Aeacidae") (i.e. his son Telamon joined [Heracles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heracles "Heracles") when he sieged the city during Laomedon's rule. Later, his great-grandson [Neoptolemus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neoptolemus "Neoptolemus") was present in the wooden horse that leads to the downfall of Troy).\[*[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed "Wikipedia:Citation needed")*]

However, the king not only refused to give the gods the wages he had promised, but also threatened to bind their feet and hands, and sell them as slaves. Angered by the unpaid labour and the insults, Apollo infected the city with a pestilence and Poseidon sent the sea monster [Cetus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cetus_\(mythology\) "Cetus (mythology)"). To deliver the city from it, Laomedon had to sacrifice his daughter [Hesione](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hesione "Hesione") (who would later be saved by [Heracles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heracles "Heracles")).\[*[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed "Wikipedia:Citation needed")*]

During his stay in Troy, Apollo had a lover named Ourea, who was a nymph and daughter of Poseidon. Together they had a son named Ileus, whom Apollo loved dearly.[\[228\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-230)

### Trojan War

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Apollo\&action=edit\&section=43 "Edit section: Trojan War")]

|                                                                                                                                                                            |                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| [![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/9/99/Question_book-new.svg/50px-Question_book-new.svg.png)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Question_book-new.svg) | This section **needs additional citations for [verification](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Verifiability "Wikipedia:Verifiability")**. Please help [improve this article](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special:EditPage/Apollo "Special:EditPage/Apollo") by [adding citations to reliable sources](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:Referencing_for_beginners "Help:Referencing for beginners") in this section. Unsourced material may be challenged and removed. *(December 2024)**&#x20;([Learn how and when to remove this message](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:Maintenance_template_removal "Help:Maintenance template removal"))* |

Apollo sided with the Trojans during the [Trojan War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trojan_War "Trojan War") waged by the Greeks against the Trojans.

During the war, the Greek king [Agamemnon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agamemnon "Agamemnon") captured [Chryseis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chryseis "Chryseis"), the daughter of Apollo's priest [Chryses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chryses "Chryses"), and refused to return her. Angered by this, Apollo shot arrows infected with the plague into the Greek encampment. He demanded that they return the girl, and the Achaeans (Greeks) complied, indirectly causing the *[anger of Achilles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Achilles_and_Patroclus#In_the_Iliad "Achilles and Patroclus")*, which is the theme of the *[Iliad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iliad "Iliad")*.

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ed/John_Flaxman_-_Apollo_Preceding_Hector_with_His_Aegis%2C_and_Dispersing_the_Greeks%2C_Fuseli%27s_Lectures_-_Google_Art_Project.jpg/220px-John_Flaxman_-_Apollo_Preceding_Hector_with_His_Aegis%2C_and_Dispersing_the_Greeks%2C_Fuseli%27s_Lectures_-_Google_Art_Project.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:John_Flaxman_-_Apollo_Preceding_Hector_with_His_Aegis,_and_Dispersing_the_Greeks,_Fuseli%27s_Lectures_-_Google_Art_Project.jpg)

Apollo preceding [Hector](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hector "Hector") with his [aegis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aegis "Aegis"), and dispersing the Greeks, by [John Flaxman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Flaxman "John Flaxman")

Receiving the [aegis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aegis "Aegis") from Zeus, Apollo entered the battlefield as per his father's command, causing great terror to the enemy with his war cry. He pushed the Greeks back and destroyed many of the soldiers. He is described as "the rouser of armies" because he rallied the Trojan army when they were falling apart.

When Zeus allowed the other gods to get involved in the war, Apollo was provoked by Poseidon to a duel. However, Apollo declined to fight him, saying that he would not fight his uncle for the sake of mortals.

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/41/Diomedes_prevented_by_Apollo_from_pursuing_Aeneas_%28%3F%29_MET_80355.jpg/220px-Diomedes_prevented_by_Apollo_from_pursuing_Aeneas_%28%3F%29_MET_80355.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Diomedes_prevented_by_Apollo_from_pursuing_Aeneas_\(%3F\)_MET_80355.jpg)

Apollo preventing [Diomedes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Diomedes "Diomedes") from pursuing [Aeneas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aeneas "Aeneas")

When the Greek hero [Diomedes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Diomedes "Diomedes") injured the Trojan hero [Aeneas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aeneas "Aeneas"), [Aphrodite](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aphrodite "Aphrodite") tried to rescue him, but Diomedes injured her as well. Apollo then enveloped Aeneas in a cloud to protect him. He repelled the attacks Diomedes made on him and gave the hero a stern warning to abstain from attacking a god. Aeneas was then taken to Pergamos, a sacred spot in [Troy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Troy "Troy"), where he was healed.

After the death of [Sarpedon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sarpedon_\(Trojan_War_hero\) "Sarpedon (Trojan War hero)"), a son of Zeus, Apollo rescued the corpse from the battlefield as per his father's wish and cleaned it. He then gave it to Sleep ([Hypnos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hypnos "Hypnos")) and Death ([Thanatos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thanatos "Thanatos")). Apollo had also once convinced Athena to stop the war for that day, so that the warriors can relieve themselves for a while.

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/be/Hector%27s_body_dragged_at_the_Chariot_of_Achilles.jpg/220px-Hector%27s_body_dragged_at_the_Chariot_of_Achilles.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Hector%27s_body_dragged_at_the_Chariot_of_Achilles.jpg)

Apollo protecting Hector's body, by [John Flaxman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Flaxman "John Flaxman")

The Trojan hero [Hector](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hector "Hector") (who, according to some, was the god's own son by [Hecuba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hecuba "Hecuba")[\[229\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-231)) was favored by Apollo. When he got severely injured, Apollo healed him and encouraged him to take up his arms. During a duel with Achilles, when Hector was about to lose, Apollo hid Hector in a cloud of mist to save him. When the Greek warrior [Patroclus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Patroclus "Patroclus") tried to get into the fort of Troy, he was stopped by Apollo. Encouraging Hector to attack Patroclus, Apollo stripped the armour of the Greek warrior and broke his weapons. Patroclus was eventually killed by Hector. At last, after Hector's fated death, Apollo protected his corpse from Achilles' attempt to mutilate it by creating a magical cloud over the corpse, shielding it from the rays of the [sun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Helios "Helios").

Apollo held a grudge against Achilles throughout the war because Achilles had murdered his son [Tenes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tenes "Tenes") before the war began and brutally assassinated his son [Troilus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Troilus "Troilus") in his own temple. Not only did Apollo save Hector from Achilles, he also tricked Achilles by disguising himself as a Trojan warrior and driving him away from the gates. He foiled Achilles' attempt to mutilate Hector's dead body.

Finally, Apollo caused Achilles' death by guiding an arrow shot by [Paris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paris_\(mythology\) "Paris (mythology)") into [Achilles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Achilles "Achilles")' heel. In some versions, Apollo himself killed Achilles by taking the disguise of Paris.

Apollo helped many Trojan warriors—including [Agenor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agenor "Agenor"), [Polydamas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polydamas_\(mythology\) "Polydamas (mythology)"), and [Glaucus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Glaucus "Glaucus")—in the battlefield. Though he greatly favored the Trojans, Apollo was bound to follow the orders of Zeus and served his father loyally during the war.

### Nurturer of the young

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Apollo\&action=edit\&section=44 "Edit section: Nurturer of the young")]

|                                                                                                                                                                            |                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| [![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/9/99/Question_book-new.svg/50px-Question_book-new.svg.png)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Question_book-new.svg) | This section **needs additional citations for [verification](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Verifiability "Wikipedia:Verifiability")**. Please help [improve this article](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special:EditPage/Apollo "Special:EditPage/Apollo") by [adding citations to reliable sources](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:Referencing_for_beginners "Help:Referencing for beginners") in this section. Unsourced material may be challenged and removed. *(December 2024)**&#x20;([Learn how and when to remove this message](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:Maintenance_template_removal "Help:Maintenance template removal"))* |

Apollo *Kourotrophos* is the god who nurtures and protects children and the young, especially boys. He oversees their education and their passage into adulthood. Education is said to have originated from Apollo and the [Muses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muses "Muses"). Many myths have him train his children. It was a custom for boys to cut and dedicate their long hair to Apollo after reaching adulthood.

[Chiron](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chiron "Chiron"), the abandoned [centaur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Centaur "Centaur"), was fostered by Apollo, who instructed him in medicine, prophecy, archery and more. Chiron would later become a great teacher himself.

[Asclepius](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asclepius "Asclepius") in his childhood gained much knowledge pertaining to medicinal arts from his father. However, he was later entrusted to Chiron for further education.

[Anius](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anius "Anius"), Apollo's son by [Rhoeo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rhoeo "Rhoeo"), was abandoned by his mother soon after his birth. Apollo brought him up and educated him in mantic arts. Anius later became the priest of Apollo and the king of Delos.

[Iamus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iamus "Iamus") was the son of Apollo and [Evadne](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Evadne "Evadne"). When Evadne went into labour, Apollo sent the [Moirai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moirai "Moirai") to assist his lover. After the child was born, Apollo sent snakes to feed the child some honey. When Iamus reached the age of education, Apollo took him to Olympia and taught him many arts, including the ability to understand and explain the languages of birds.[\[230\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-232)

[Idmon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Idmon "Idmon") was educated by Apollo to be a seer. Even though he foresaw his death that would happen in his journey with the [Argonauts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Argonauts "Argonauts"), he embraced his destiny and died a brave death. To commemorate his son's bravery, Apollo commanded Boeotians to build a town around the tomb of the hero, and to honor him.[\[231\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-233)

Apollo adopted [Carnus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carnus "Carnus"), the abandoned son of Zeus and [Europa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Europa_\(consort_of_Zeus\) "Europa (consort of Zeus)"). He reared the child with the help of his mother Leto and educated him to be a seer.

When his son [Melaneus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Melaneus_of_Oechalia "Melaneus of Oechalia") reached the age of marriage, Apollo asked the princess [Stratonice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stratonice_\(mythology\) "Stratonice (mythology)") to be his son's bride and carried her away from her home when she agreed.

Apollo saved a shepherd boy (name unknown) from death in a large deep cave, by means of vultures. To thank him, the shepherd built Apollo a temple under the name Vulturius.[\[232\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-234)

### God of music

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Apollo\&action=edit\&section=45 "Edit section: God of music")]

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7f/The_music_of_the_spheres.jpg/220px-The_music_of_the_spheres.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:The_music_of_the_spheres.jpg)

The music of the spheres. Shown in this engraving from Renaissance Italy are Apollo, the Muses, the planetary spheres and musical ratios.

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/73/Apollo%2C_Hyacinthus_and_Cyparis_singing_and_playing_by_Alexander_Ivanov.jpg/220px-Apollo%2C_Hyacinthus_and_Cyparis_singing_and_playing_by_Alexander_Ivanov.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Apollo,_Hyacinthus_and_Cyparis_singing_and_playing_by_Alexander_Ivanov.jpg)

Apollo, [Hyacinth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hyacinth_\(mythology\) "Hyacinth (mythology)") and [Cyparissus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cyparissus "Cyparissus") singing and playing, by [Alexander Ivanov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexander_Andreyevich_Ivanov "Alexander Andreyevich Ivanov") 1831–1834

Immediately after his birth, Apollo demanded a lyre and invented the [paean](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paean "Paean"), thus becoming the god of music. As the divine singer, he is the patron of poets, singers and musicians. The invention of string music is attributed to him. [Plato](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plato "Plato") said that the innate ability of humans to take delight in music, rhythm and harmony is the gift of Apollo and the Muses.[\[233\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-235) According to [Socrates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Socrates "Socrates"), ancient Greeks believed that Apollo is the god who directs the harmony and makes all things move together, both for the gods and the humans. For this reason, he was called *Homopolon* before the *Homo* was replaced by *A*.[\[234\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-236)[\[235\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-237) Apollo's harmonious music delivered people from their pain, and hence, like Dionysus, he is also called the liberator.[\[159\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-ReferenceA-161) The swans, which were considered to be the most musical among the birds, were believed to be the "singers of Apollo". They are Apollo's sacred birds and acted as his vehicle during his travel to [Hyperborea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hyperborea "Hyperborea").[\[159\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-ReferenceA-161) [Aelian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Claudius_Aelianus "Claudius Aelianus") says that when the singers would sing hymns to Apollo, the swans would join the chant in unison.[\[236\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-238)

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/97/Parnassus%2C_Andrea_Appiani_%281811%29.jpg/270px-Parnassus%2C_Andrea_Appiani_%281811%29.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Parnassus,_Andrea_Appiani_\(1811\).jpg)

Apollo and the Muses on Parnassus, by [Andrea Appiani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andrea_Appiani "Andrea Appiani")

Among the [Pythagoreans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pythagoreanism "Pythagoreanism"), the study of mathematics and music were connected to the worship of Apollo, their principal deity.[\[237\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-239)[\[238\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-240)[\[239\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-241) Their belief was that music purifies the soul, just as medicine purifies the body. They also believed that music was delegated to the same mathematical laws of harmony as the mechanics of the cosmos, evolving into an idea known as the [music of the spheres](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Music_of_the_spheres "Music of the spheres").[\[240\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-242)

Apollo appears as the companion of the [Muses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muses "Muses"), and as [Musagetes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Musagetes "Musagetes") ("leader of Muses") he leads them in dance. They spend their time on [Parnassus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parnassus "Parnassus"), which is one of their sacred places. Apollo is also the lover of the Muses and by them he became the father of famous musicians like [Orpheus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Orpheus "Orpheus") and [Linus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Linus_of_Thrace "Linus of Thrace").\[*[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed "Wikipedia:Citation needed")*]

Apollo is often found delighting the immortal gods with his songs and music on the [lyre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lyre "Lyre").[\[241\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-243) In his role as the god of banquets, he was always present to play music at weddings of the gods, like the marriage of [Eros](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eros "Eros") and [Psyche](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cupid_and_Psyche "Cupid and Psyche"), [Peleus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peleus "Peleus") and [Thetis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thetis "Thetis"). He is a frequent guest of the [Bacchanalia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bacchanalia "Bacchanalia"), and many ancient ceramics depict him being at ease amidst the [maenads](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maenads "Maenads") and satyrs.[\[242\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-244) Apollo also participated in musical contests when challenged by others. He was the victor in all those contests, but he tended to punish his opponents severely for their [hubris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hubris "Hubris").\[*[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed "Wikipedia:Citation needed")*]

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/ba/Detail_of_the_statue_of_Apollo_holding_the_kithara%2C_from_the_Temple_of_Venus_%28Casino_Fede%29_at_Hadrian%27s_Villa%2C_Ny_Carlsberg_Glyptoteket_%2812233881783%29.jpg/170px-Detail_of_the_statue_of_Apollo_holding_the_kithara%2C_from_the_Temple_of_Venus_%28Casino_Fede%29_at_Hadrian%27s_Villa%2C_Ny_Carlsberg_Glyptoteket_%2812233881783%29.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Detail_of_the_statue_of_Apollo_holding_the_kithara,_from_the_Temple_of_Venus_\(Casino_Fede\)_at_Hadrian%27s_Villa,_Ny_Carlsberg_Glyptoteket_\(12233881783\).jpg)

Detail of Apollo's lyre

#### Apollo's lyre

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Apollo\&action=edit\&section=46 "Edit section: Apollo's lyre")]

The invention of the lyre is attributed either to [Hermes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hermes "Hermes") or to Apollo himself.[\[243\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-245) Distinctions have been made that Hermes invented lyre made of tortoise shell, whereas the lyre Apollo invented was a regular lyre.[\[244\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-246)

Myths tell that the infant Hermes stole a number of Apollo's cows and took them to a cave in the woods near [Pylos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pylos "Pylos"), covering their tracks. In the cave, he found a [tortoise](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tortoise "Tortoise") and killed it, then removed the insides. He used one of the cow's intestines and the tortoise shell and made his [lyre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lyre "Lyre").\[*[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed "Wikipedia:Citation needed")*]

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/15/No%C3%ABl_Coypel_-_Story_of_Apollo-Apollo_and_Mercury%2C_1688.jpg/180px-No%C3%ABl_Coypel_-_Story_of_Apollo-Apollo_and_Mercury%2C_1688.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:No%C3%ABl_Coypel_-_Story_of_Apollo-Apollo_and_Mercury,_1688.jpg)

The friendship of Apollo and Hermes, by [Noël Coypel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/No%C3%ABl_Coypel "Noël Coypel")

Upon discovering the theft, Apollo confronted Hermes and asked him to return his cattle. When Hermes acted innocent, Apollo took the matter to Zeus. Zeus, having seen the events, sided with Apollo, and ordered Hermes to return the cattle.[\[245\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-247) Hermes then began to play music on the lyre he had invented. Apollo fell in love with the instrument and offered to exchange the cattle for the lyre. Hence, Apollo then became the master of the lyre.\[*[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed "Wikipedia:Citation needed")*]

According to other versions, Apollo had invented the lyre himself, whose strings he tore in repenting of the excess punishment he had given to [Marsyas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marsyas "Marsyas"). Hermes' lyre, therefore, would be a reinvention.[\[246\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-248)

#### Contest with Pan

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Apollo\&action=edit\&section=47 "Edit section: Contest with Pan")]

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5a/Laurits_Regner_Tuxen_The-Musical-Dual-Of-Pan-And-Apollo.jpg/220px-Laurits_Regner_Tuxen_The-Musical-Dual-Of-Pan-And-Apollo.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Laurits_Regner_Tuxen_The-Musical-Dual-Of-Pan-And-Apollo.jpg)

The musical duel of Pan and Apollo, by [Laurits Tuxen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Laurits_Tuxen "Laurits Tuxen")

Once [Pan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pan_\(mythology\) "Pan (mythology)") had the audacity to compare his music with that of Apollo and to challenge the god of music to a contest. The mountain-god [Tmolus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tmolus_\(son_of_Ares\) "Tmolus (son of Ares)") was chosen to umpire. Pan blew on his pipes, and with his rustic melody gave great satisfaction to himself and his faithful follower, [Midas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Midas "Midas"), who happened to be present. Then, Apollo struck the strings of his lyre. It was so beautiful that Tmolus at once awarded the victory to Apollo, and everyone was pleased with the judgement. Only Midas dissented and questioned the justice of the award. Apollo did not want to suffer such a depraved pair of ears any longer, and caused them to become the ears of a donkey.\[*[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed "Wikipedia:Citation needed")*]

#### Contest with Marsyas

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Apollo\&action=edit\&section=48 "Edit section: Contest with Marsyas")]

[Marsyas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marsyas "Marsyas") was a [satyr](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Satyr "Satyr") who was punished by Apollo for his [hubris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hubris "Hubris"). He had found an [aulos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aulos "Aulos") on the ground, tossed away after being invented by [Athena](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Athena "Athena") because it made her cheeks puffy. Athena had also placed a curse upon the instrument, that whoever would pick it up would be severely punished. When Marsyas played the flute, everyone became frenzied with joy. This led Marsyas to think that he was better than Apollo, and he challenged the god to a musical contest. The contest was judged by the [Muses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muse "Muse"), or the nymphs of [Nysa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nysa_\(mythology\) "Nysa (mythology)"). Athena was also present to witness the contest.\[*[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed "Wikipedia:Citation needed")*]

Marsyas taunted Apollo for "wearing his hair long, for having a fair face and smooth body, for his skill in so many arts".[\[247\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Apuleius,_Florida_3.2-249) He also further said,

> 'His \[Apollo] hair is smooth and made into tufts and curls that fall about his brow and hang before his face. His body is fair from head to foot, his limbs shine bright, his tongue gives oracles, and he is equally eloquent in prose or verse, propose which you will. What of his robes so fine in texture, so soft to the touch, aglow with purple? What of his lyre that flashes gold, gleams white with ivory, and shimmers with rainbow gems? What of his song, so cunning and so sweet? Nay, all these allurements suit with naught save luxury. To virtue they bring shame alone!'[\[247\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Apuleius,_Florida_3.2-249)

The Muses and Athena sniggered at this comment. The contestants agreed to take turns displaying their skills and the rule was that the victor could "do whatever he wanted" to the loser.\[*[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed "Wikipedia:Citation needed")*]

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/70/05apol1.jpg/260px-05apol1.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:05apol1.jpg)

The contest between Apollo and [Marsyas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marsyas "Marsyas"), by [Palma il Giovane](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palma_il_Giovane "Palma il Giovane")

According to one account, after the first round, they both were deemed equal by the [Nysiads](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nysiads "Nysiads"). But in the next round, Apollo decided to play on his lyre and add his melodious voice to his performance. Marsyas argued against this, saying that Apollo would have an advantage and accused Apollo of cheating. But Apollo replied that since Marsyas played the flute, which needed air blown from the throat, it was similar to singing, and that either they both should get an equal chance to combine their skills or none of them should use their mouths at all.
