The nymphs decided that Apollo's argument was just. Apollo then played his lyre and sang at the same time, mesmerising the audience. Marsyas could not do this. Apollo was declared the winner and, angered with Marsyas' haughtiness and his accusations, decided to flay the satyr.[\[248\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-ReferenceD-250)

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/53/Marsyas_Flayed_by_the_Order_of_Apollo_-_Charles_Andr%C3%A9_van_Loo_%281735%29.jpg/220px-Marsyas_Flayed_by_the_Order_of_Apollo_-_Charles_Andr%C3%A9_van_Loo_%281735%29.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Marsyas_Flayed_by_the_Order_of_Apollo_-_Charles_Andr%C3%A9_van_Loo_\(1735\).jpg)

Marsyas flayed by the order of Apollo, by [Charles-André van Loo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charles-Andr%C3%A9_van_Loo "Charles-André van Loo")

According to another account, Marsyas played his flute out of tune at one point and accepted his defeat. Out of shame, he assigned to himself the punishment of being skinned for a wine sack.[\[249\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-251) Another variation is that Apollo played his instrument upside down. Marsyas could not do this with his instrument. So the Muses who were the judges declared Apollo the winner. Apollo hung Marsyas from a tree to flay him.[\[250\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-252)

Apollo [flayed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flaying "Flaying") the limbs of Marsyas alive in a cave near [Celaenae](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Celaenae "Celaenae") in [Phrygia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Phrygia "Phrygia") for his [hubris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hubris "Hubris") to challenge a god. He then gave the rest of his body for proper burial[\[251\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-253) and nailed Marsyas' flayed skin to a nearby pine-tree as a lesson to the others. Marsyas' blood turned into the river Marsyas. But Apollo soon repented and being distressed at what he had done, he tore the strings of his lyre and threw it away. The lyre was later discovered by the Muses and Apollo's sons [Linus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Linus_of_Thrace "Linus of Thrace") and [Orpheus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Orpheus "Orpheus"). The Muses fixed the middle string, Linus the string struck with the forefinger, and Orpheus the lowest string and the one next to it. They took it back to Apollo, but the god, who had decided to stay away from music for a while, laid away both the lyre and the pipes at Delphi and joined [Cybele](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cybele "Cybele") in her wanderings to as far as [Hyperborea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hyperborea "Hyperborea").[\[248\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-ReferenceD-250)[\[252\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-254)

#### Contest with Cinyras

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Apollo\&action=edit\&section=49 "Edit section: Contest with Cinyras")]

Cinyras was a ruler of [Cyprus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cyprus "Cyprus"), who was a friend of [Agamemnon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agamemnon "Agamemnon"). Cinyras promised to assist Agamemnon in the Trojan war, but did not keep his promise. Agamemnon cursed Cinyras. He invoked Apollo and asked the god to avenge the broken promise. Apollo then had a [lyre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lyre "Lyre")-playing contest with [Cinyras](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cinyras "Cinyras"), and defeated him. Either Cinyras committed suicide when he lost, or was killed by Apollo.[\[253\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-255)[\[254\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-256)

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e7/Apollon_Raon_Versailles_MV5921.jpg/220px-Apollon_Raon_Versailles_MV5921.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Apollon_Raon_Versailles_MV5921.jpg)

Apollon Raon, [Versailles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Versailles "Versailles")

### Patron of sailors

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Apollo\&action=edit\&section=50 "Edit section: Patron of sailors")]

Apollo functions as the patron and protector of sailors, one of the duties he shares with [Poseidon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Poseidon "Poseidon"). In the myths, he is seen helping heroes who pray to him for a safe journey.\[*[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed "Wikipedia:Citation needed")*]

When Apollo spotted a ship of Cretan sailors that were caught in a storm, he quickly assumed the shape of a dolphin and guided their ship safely to Delphi.[\[255\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-257)

When the [Argonauts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Argonauts "Argonauts") faced a terrible storm, [Jason](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jason "Jason") prayed to his patron, Apollo, to help them. Apollo used his bow and golden arrow to shed light upon an island, where the Argonauts soon took shelter. This island was renamed "[Anaphe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anafi "Anafi")", which means "He revealed it".[\[256\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-258)

Apollo helped the Greek hero [Diomedes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Diomedes "Diomedes"), to escape from a great tempest during his journey homeward. As a token of gratitude, Diomedes built a temple in honor of Apollo under the epithet Epibaterius ("the embarker").[\[257\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-259)

During the Trojan War, [Odysseus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Odysseus "Odysseus") came to the Trojan camp to return Chriseis, the daughter of Apollo's priest [Chryses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chryses "Chryses"), and brought many offerings to Apollo. Pleased with this, Apollo sent gentle breezes that helped Odysseus return safely to the Greek camp.[\[258\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-260)

[Arion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arion "Arion") was a poet who was kidnapped by some sailors for the rich prizes he possessed. Arion requested them to let him sing for the last time, to which the sailors consented. Arion began singing a song in praise of Apollo, seeking the god's help. Consequently, numerous dolphins surrounded the ship and when Arion jumped into the water, the dolphins carried him away safely.\[*[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed "Wikipedia:Citation needed")*]

### Wars

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Apollo\&action=edit\&section=51 "Edit section: Wars")]

#### Trojan War

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Apollo\&action=edit\&section=52 "Edit section: Trojan War")]

Apollo played a pivotal role in the entire Trojan War. He sided with the Trojans, and sent a terrible plague to the Greek camp, which indirectly led to the conflict between [Achilles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Achilles "Achilles") and [Agamemnon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agamemnon "Agamemnon"). He killed the Greek heroes [Patroclus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Patroclus "Patroclus"), Achilles, and numerous Greek soldiers. He also helped many Trojan heroes, the most important one being [Hector](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hector "Hector"). After the end of the war, Apollo and Poseidon together cleaned the remains of the city and the camps.\[*[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed "Wikipedia:Citation needed")*]

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a3/Paris_armour_Pomarici_Santomasi.jpg/220px-Paris_armour_Pomarici_Santomasi.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Paris_armour_Pomarici_Santomasi.jpg)

Paris (on the left) putting on his armour as Apollo (on the right) watches him. Attic red-figure kantharos, 425–420 BC

#### Telegony war

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Apollo\&action=edit\&section=53 "Edit section: Telegony war")]

A war broke out between the [Brygoi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brygoi "Brygoi") and the Thesprotians, who had the support of [Odysseus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Odysseus "Odysseus"). The gods Athena and [Ares](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ares "Ares") came to the battlefield and took sides. Athena helped the hero Odysseus while Ares fought alongside of the Brygoi. When Odysseus lost, Athena and Ares came into a direct duel. To stop the battling gods and the terror created by their battle, Apollo intervened and stopped the duel between them.[\[259\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-261)[\[260\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-262)

#### Indian war

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Apollo\&action=edit\&section=54 "Edit section: Indian war")]

When Zeus suggested that [Dionysus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dionysus "Dionysus") defeat the Indians in order to earn a place among the gods, Dionysus declared war against the Indians and travelled to India along with his army of [Bacchantes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bacchantes "Bacchantes") and [satyrs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Satyrs "Satyrs"). Among the warriors was [Aristaeus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aristaeus "Aristaeus"), Apollo's son. Apollo armed his son with his own hands and gave him a bow and arrows and fitted a strong shield to his arm.[\[261\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-263) After Zeus urged Apollo to join the war, he went to the battlefield.[\[262\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-264) Seeing several of his [nymphs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nymphs "Nymphs") and Aristaeus drowning in a river, he took them to safety and healed them.[\[263\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-265) He taught Aristaeus more useful healing arts and sent him back to help the army of Dionysus.\[*[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed "Wikipedia:Citation needed")*]

#### Theban war

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Apollo\&action=edit\&section=55 "Edit section: Theban war")]

During the war between the sons of [Oedipus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oedipus "Oedipus"), Apollo favored [Amphiaraus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amphiaraus "Amphiaraus"), a seer and one of the leaders in the war. Though saddened that the seer was fated to be doomed in the war, Apollo made Amphiaraus' last hours glorious by "lighting his shield and his helm with starry gleam". When [Hypseus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hypseus "Hypseus") tried to kill the hero with a spear, Apollo directed the spear towards the charioteer of Amphiaraus instead. Then Apollo himself replaced the charioteer and took the reins in his hands. He deflected many spears and arrows away from them. He also killed many of the enemy warriors like [Melaneus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Melaneus_\(mythology\) "Melaneus (mythology)"), [Antiphus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antiphus "Antiphus"), Aetion, Polites and [Lampus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lampus "Lampus"). At last, when the moment of departure came, Apollo expressed his grief with tears in his eyes and bid farewell to Amphiaraus, who was soon engulfed by the Earth.[\[264\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-266)

### Slaying of giants

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Apollo\&action=edit\&section=56 "Edit section: Slaying of giants")]

Apollo killed the giants Python and Tityos, who had assaulted his mother Leto.\[*[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed "Wikipedia:Citation needed")*]

#### Gigantomachy

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Apollo\&action=edit\&section=57 "Edit section: Gigantomachy")]

During the [gigantomachy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gigantomachy "Gigantomachy"), Apollo and [Heracles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heracles "Heracles") blinded the giant [Ephialtes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ephialtes_\(disambiguation\) "Ephialtes (disambiguation)") by shooting him in his eyes, Apollo shooting his left and Heracles his right.[\[265\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-267) He also killed [Porphyrion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Porphyrion "Porphyrion"), the king of giants, using his bow and arrows.[\[266\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-268)

#### Aloadae

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Apollo\&action=edit\&section=58 "Edit section: Aloadae")]

The [Aloadae](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aloadae "Aloadae"), namely Otis and Ephialtes, were twin giants who decided to wage war upon the gods. They attempted to storm Mt. Olympus by piling up mountains, and threatened to fill the sea with mountains and inundate dry land.[\[267\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-269) They even dared to seek the hand of Hera and Artemis in marriage. Angered by this, Apollo killed them by shooting them with arrows.[\[268\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-270) According to another tale, Apollo killed them by sending a deer between them; as they tried to kill it with their javelins, they accidentally stabbed each other and died.[\[269\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-271)

#### Phorbas

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Apollo\&action=edit\&section=59 "Edit section: Phorbas")]

[Phorbas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Phorbas "Phorbas") was a savage giant king of [Phlegyas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Phlegyas_\(Boeotia\) "Phlegyas (Boeotia)") who was described as having swine-like features. He wished to plunder Delphi for its wealth. He seized the roads to Delphi and started harassing the pilgrims. He captured the old people and children and sent them to his army to hold them for ransom. And he challenged the young and sturdy men to a match of boxing, only to cut their heads off when they would get defeated by him. He hung the chopped-off heads to an oak tree. Finally, Apollo came to put an end to this cruelty. He entered a boxing contest with Phorbas and killed him with a single blow.[\[270\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-272)

### Other stories

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Apollo\&action=edit\&section=60 "Edit section: Other stories")]

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5a/Fran%C3%A7ois_Boucher_-_The_Rising_of_the_Sun_-_WGA02916.jpg/220px-Fran%C3%A7ois_Boucher_-_The_Rising_of_the_Sun_-_WGA02916.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Fran%C3%A7ois_Boucher_-_The_Rising_of_the_Sun_-_WGA02916.jpg)

Apollo as the rising sun, by [François Boucher](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fran%C3%A7ois_Boucher "François Boucher")

In the first [Olympic games](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Olympic_games "Olympic games"), Apollo defeated [Ares](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ares "Ares") and became the victor in wrestling. He outran [Hermes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hermes "Hermes") in the race and won first place.[\[271\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-273)

Apollo divides months into summer and winter.[\[272\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-274) He rides on the back of a swan to the land of the [Hyperboreans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hyperborea "Hyperborea") during the winter months, and the absence of warmth in winter is due to his departure. During his absence, Delphi was under the care of [Dionysus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dionysus "Dionysus"), and no prophecies were given during winters.\[*[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed "Wikipedia:Citation needed")*]

#### Periphas

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Apollo\&action=edit\&section=61 "Edit section: Periphas")]

[Periphas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Periphas "Periphas") was an Attican king and a priest of Apollo. He was noble, just and rich. He did all his duties justly. Because of this people were very fond of him and started honouring him to the same extent as Zeus. At one point, they worshipped Periphas in place of Zeus and set up shrines and temples for him. This annoyed Zeus, who decided to annihilate the entire family of Periphas. But because he was a just king and a good devotee, Apollo intervened and requested his father to spare Periphas. Zeus considered Apollo's words and agreed to let him live. But he metamorphosed Periphas into an eagle and made the eagle the king of birds. When Periphas' wife requested Zeus to let her stay with her husband, Zeus turned her into a vulture and fulfilled her wish.[\[273\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-275)

#### Molpadia and Parthenos

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Apollo\&action=edit\&section=62 "Edit section: Molpadia and Parthenos")]

Molpadia and Parthenos were the sisters of [Rhoeo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rhoeo "Rhoeo"), a former lover of Apollo. One day, they were put in charge of watching their father's ancestral wine jar but they fell asleep while performing this duty. While they were asleep, the wine jar was broken by the swine their family kept. When the sisters woke up and saw what had happened, they threw themselves off a cliff in fear of their father's wrath. Apollo, who was passing by, caught them and carried them to two different cities in Chersonesus, Molpadia to Castabus and Parthenos to Bubastus. He turned them into goddesses and they both received divine honors. Molpadia's name was changed to [Hemithea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hemithea_\(mythology\) "Hemithea (mythology)") upon her deification.[\[274\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-276)

#### Prometheus

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Apollo\&action=edit\&section=63 "Edit section: Prometheus")]

[Prometheus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prometheus "Prometheus") was the titan who was punished by Zeus for stealing fire. He was bound to a rock, where each day an eagle was sent to eat Prometheus' liver, which would then grow back overnight to be eaten again the next day. Seeing his plight, Apollo pleaded with Zeus to release the kind Titan, while Artemis and Leto stood behind him with tears in their eyes. Zeus, moved by Apollo's words and the tears of the goddesses, finally sent Heracles to free Prometheus.[\[275\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-277)

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/56/Nicolas-Guy_Brenet_-_Apollo_Crowning_the_Arts%2C_1771.jpg/220px-Nicolas-Guy_Brenet_-_Apollo_Crowning_the_Arts%2C_1771.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Nicolas-Guy_Brenet_-_Apollo_Crowning_the_Arts,_1771.jpg)

Apollo crowning the arts, by [Nicolas-Guy Brenet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nicolas-Guy_Brenet "Nicolas-Guy Brenet")

#### Heracles

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Apollo\&action=edit\&section=64 "Edit section: Heracles")]

After [Heracles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heracles "Heracles") (then named Alcides) was struck with madness and killed his family, he sought to purify himself and consulted the oracle of Apollo. Apollo, through the Pythia, commanded him to serve king [Eurystheus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eurystheus "Eurystheus") for twelve years and complete the ten tasks the king would give him. Only then would Alcides be absolved of his sin. Apollo also renamed him Heracles.[\[276\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-278)

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/0c/Corinthian_helmet_Cdm_Paris_BB2013_n2.jpg/220px-Corinthian_helmet_Cdm_Paris_BB2013_n2.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Corinthian_helmet_Cdm_Paris_BB2013_n2.jpg)

Heracles and Apollo struggling over the hind, as depicted on a Corinthian helmet (early 5th century BC)

To complete his third task, Heracles had to capture the [Ceryneian Hind](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ceryneian_Hind "Ceryneian Hind"), a hind sacred to Artemis, and bring back it alive. After chasing the hind for one year, the animal eventually got tired, and when it tried crossing the river Ladon, Heracles captured it. While he was taking it back, he was confronted by Apollo and Artemis, who were angered at Heracles for this act. However, Heracles soothed the goddess and explained his situation to her. After much pleading, Artemis permitted him to take the hind and told him to return it later.[\[277\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-279)

After he was freed from his servitude to Eurystheus, Heracles fell in conflict with Iphytus, a prince of Oechalia, and murdered him. Soon after, he contracted a terrible disease. He consulted the oracle of Apollo once again, in the hope of ridding himself of the disease. The Pythia, however, denied to give any prophesy. In anger, Heracles snatched the sacred tripod and started walking away, intending to start his own oracle. However, Apollo did not tolerate this and stopped Heracles; a duel ensued between them. Artemis rushed to support Apollo, while Athena supported Heracles. Soon, Zeus threw his thunderbolt between the fighting brothers and separated them. He reprimanded Heracles for this act of violation and asked Apollo to give a solution to Heracles. Apollo then ordered the hero to serve under [Omphale](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Omphale "Omphale"), queen of [Lydia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lydia "Lydia") for one year in order to purify himself.\[*[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed "Wikipedia:Citation needed")*]

After their reconciliation, Apollo and Heracles together founded the city of Gythion.[\[278\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-280)

#### Plato's concept of soulmates

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Apollo\&action=edit\&section=65 "Edit section: Plato's concept of soulmates")]

A long time ago, there were three kinds of human beings: male, descended from the sun; female, descended from the earth; and androgynous, descended from the moon. Each human being was completely round, with four arms and four legs, two identical faces on opposite sides of a head with four ears, and all else to match. They were powerful and unruly. [Otis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aloadae "Aloadae") and [Ephialtes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aloadae "Aloadae") even dared to scale [Mount Olympus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mount_Olympus "Mount Olympus").\[*[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed "Wikipedia:Citation needed")*]

To check their insolence, Zeus devised a plan to humble them and improve their manners instead of completely destroying them. He cut them all in two and asked Apollo to make necessary repairs, giving humans the individual shape they still have now. Apollo turned their heads and necks around towards their wounds, he pulled together their skin at the [abdomen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abdomen "Abdomen"), and sewed the skin together at the middle of it. This is what we call [navel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Navel "Navel") today. He smoothened the wrinkles and shaped the chest. But he made sure to leave a few wrinkles on the abdomen and around the navel so that they might be reminded of their punishment.[\[279\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-281)

> "As he \[Zeus] cut them one after another, he bade Apollo give the face and the half of the neck a turn... Apollo was also bidden to heal their wounds and compose their forms. So Apollo gave a turn to the face and pulled the skin from the sides all over that which in our language is called the belly, like the purses which draw in, and he made one mouth at the centre \[of the belly] which he fastened in a knot (the same which is called the navel); he also moulded the breast and took out most of the wrinkles, much as a shoemaker might smooth leather upon a last; he left a few wrinkles, however, in the region of the belly and navel, as a memorial of the primeval state.

#### The rock of Leukas

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Apollo\&action=edit\&section=66 "Edit section: The rock of Leukas")]

Leukatas was believed to be a white-colored rock jutting out from the island of [Leukas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lefkada "Lefkada") into the sea. It was present in the sanctuary of Apollo Leukates. A leap from this rock was believed to have put an end to the longings of love.[\[280\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Strabo,_Geography_10._2._8-282)

Once, Aphrodite fell deeply in love with [Adonis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adonis "Adonis"), a young man of great beauty who was later accidentally killed by a boar. Heartbroken, Aphrodite wandered looking for the rock of Leukas. When she reached the sanctuary of Apollo in Argos, she confided in him her love and sorrow. Apollo then brought her to the rock of Leukas and asked her to throw herself from the top of the rock. She did so and was freed from her love. When she sought the reason behind this, Apollo told her that Zeus, before taking another lover, would sit on this rock to free himself from his love for Hera.[\[210\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-ReferenceE-212)

Another tale relates that a man named Nireus, who fell in love with the cult statue of Athena, came to the rock and jumped in order to relieve himself. After jumping, he fell into the net of a fisherman in which, when he was pulled out, he found a box filled with gold. He fought with the fisherman and took the gold, but Apollo appeared to him in the night in a dream and warned him not to appropriate gold which belonged to others.[\[210\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-ReferenceE-212)

It was an ancestral custom among the Leukadians to fling a criminal from this rock every year at the sacrifice performed in honor of Apollo for the sake of averting evil. However, a number of men would be stationed all around below rock to catch the criminal and take him out of the borders in order to exile him from the island.[\[281\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-283)[\[210\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-ReferenceE-212) This was the same rock from which, according to a legend, Sappho took her suicidal leap.[\[280\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Strabo,_Geography_10._2._8-282)

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b9/Fran%C3%A7ois_Boucher_028.jpg/220px-Fran%C3%A7ois_Boucher_028.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Fran%C3%A7ois_Boucher_028.jpg)

Apollo as the setting sun, by [François Boucher](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fran%C3%A7ois_Boucher "François Boucher")

#### Slaying of Titans

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Apollo\&action=edit\&section=67 "Edit section: Slaying of Titans")]

Once [Hera](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hera "Hera"), out of spite, aroused the Titans to war against [Zeus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zeus "Zeus") and take away his throne. Accordingly, when the Titans tried to climb [Mount Olympus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mount_Olympus "Mount Olympus"), Zeus with the help of Apollo, [Artemis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Artemis "Artemis") and [Athena](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Athena "Athena"), defeated them and cast them into Tartarus.[\[282\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-284)

### Female lovers

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Apollo\&action=edit\&section=68 "Edit section: Female lovers")]

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/28/Robert_Sanderson_-_Apollo_and_the_Muses.jpg/220px-Robert_Sanderson_-_Apollo_and_the_Muses.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Robert_Sanderson_-_Apollo_and_the_Muses.jpg)

Apollo and the Muses, by Robert Sanderson

Apollo is said to have been the lover of all nine [Muses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muses "Muses"), and not being able to choose one of them, he decided to remain unwed. He fathered the [Corybantes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Corybantes "Corybantes") by the Muse [Thalia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thalia_\(Muse\) "Thalia (Muse)").[\[283\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-285) By [Calliope](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Calliope "Calliope"), he had [Hymenaios](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hymenaios "Hymenaios"), [Ialemus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ialemus "Ialemus"), [Orpheus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Orpheus "Orpheus")[\[284\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-286) and [Linus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Linus_\(Thracian\) "Linus (Thracian)"). Alternatively, Linus was said to be the son of Apollo and either [Urania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Urania "Urania") or [Terpsichore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Terpsichore "Terpsichore").\[*[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed "Wikipedia:Citation needed")*]

In the Great Eoiae that is attributed to [Hesiod](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hesiod "Hesiod"), Scylla is the daughter of Apollo and Hecate.[\[285\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-287)

[Cyrene](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cyrene_\(mythology\) "Cyrene (mythology)") was a Thessalian princess whom Apollo loved. In her honor, he built the city Cyrene and made her its ruler. She was later granted longevity by Apollo who turned her into a nymph. The couple had two sons, [Aristaeus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aristaeus "Aristaeus"), and [Idmon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Idmon "Idmon").\[*[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed "Wikipedia:Citation needed")*]

[Evadne](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Evadne "Evadne") was a nymph daughter of Poseidon and a lover of Apollo. They had a son, [Iamos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iamos "Iamos"). During the time of the childbirth, Apollo sent [Eileithyia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eileithyia "Eileithyia"), the goddess of childbirth to assist her.

[Rhoeo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rhoeo "Rhoeo"), a princess of the island of Naxos was loved by Apollo. Out of affection for her, Apollo turned her sisters into goddesses. On the island Delos she bore Apollo a son named [Anius](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anius "Anius"). Not wanting to have the child, she entrusted the infant to Apollo and left. Apollo raised and educated the child on his own.\[*[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed "Wikipedia:Citation needed")*]

Ourea, a daughter of [Poseidon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Poseidon "Poseidon"), fell in love with Apollo when he and Poseidon were serving the Trojan king [Laomedon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Laomedon "Laomedon"). They both united on the day the walls of [Troy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Troy "Troy") were built. She bore to Apollo a son, whom Apollo named Ileus, after the city of his birth, Ilion ([Troy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Troy "Troy")). Ileus was very dear to Apollo.[\[286\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-288)

[Thero](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thero_\(mythology\) "Thero (mythology)"), daughter of [Phylas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Phylas "Phylas"), a maiden as beautiful as the moonbeams, was loved by the radiant Apollo, and she loved him in return. Through their union, she became the mother of Chaeron, who was famed as "the tamer of horses". He later built the city [Chaeronea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chaeronea "Chaeronea").[\[287\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-289)

Hyrie or Thyrie was the mother of [Cycnus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cycnus_\(son_of_Apollo\) "Cycnus (son of Apollo)"). Apollo turned both the mother and son into swans when they jumped into a lake and tried to kill themselves.[\[288\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-290)

[Hecuba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hecuba "Hecuba") was the wife of King [Priam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Priam "Priam") of [Troy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Troy "Troy"), and Apollo had a son with her named [Troilus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Troilus "Troilus"). An [oracle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oracle "Oracle") prophesied that Troy would not be defeated as long as Troilus reached the age of twenty alive. He was ambushed and killed by [Achilleus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Achilleus "Achilleus"), and Apollo avenged his death by killing Achilles. After the sack of Troy, Hecuba was taken to Lycia by Apollo.[\[289\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-291)

[Coronis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coronis_\(lover_of_Apollo\) "Coronis (lover of Apollo)") was daughter of [Phlegyas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Phlegyas "Phlegyas"), King of the [Lapiths](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lapiths "Lapiths"). While pregnant with [Asclepius](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asclepius "Asclepius"), Coronis fell in love with [Ischys](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ischys "Ischys"), son of [Elatus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elatus "Elatus") and slept with him. When Apollo found out about her infidelity through his prophetic powers or thanks to his [raven](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lycius_\(son_of_Clinis\) "Lycius (son of Clinis)") who informed him, he sent his sister, Artemis, to kill Coronis. Apollo rescued the baby by cutting open Coronis' belly and gave it to the [centaur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Centaur "Centaur") [Chiron](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chiron "Chiron") to raise.\[*[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed "Wikipedia:Citation needed")*]

[Dryope](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dryope_\(daughter_of_Dryops\) "Dryope (daughter of Dryops)"), the daughter of Dryops, was impregnated by Apollo in the form of a snake. She gave birth to a son named Amphissus.[\[290\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-292)

In [Euripides](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Euripides "Euripides")' play *[Ion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ion_\(play\) "Ion (play)")*, Apollo fathered [Ion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ion_\(mythology\) "Ion (mythology)") by [Creusa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Creusa_\(daughter_of_Erechtheus\) "Creusa (daughter of Erechtheus)"), wife of [Xuthus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Xuthus "Xuthus"). He used his powers to conceal her pregnancy from her father. Later, when Creusa left Ion to die in the wild, Apollo asked [Hermes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hermes "Hermes") to save the child and bring him to the oracle at [Delphi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delphi "Delphi"), where he was raised by a priestess.\[*[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed "Wikipedia:Citation needed")*]

Apollo loved and kidnapped an Oceanid nymph, [Melia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Melia_\(consort_of_Apollo\) "Melia (consort of Apollo)"). Her father [Oceanus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oceanus "Oceanus") sent one of his sons, [Caanthus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caanthus "Caanthus"), to find her, but Caanthus could not take her back from Apollo, so he burned Apollo's sanctuary. In retaliation, Apollo shot and killed Caanthus.[\[291\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-293)

### Male lovers

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Apollo\&action=edit\&section=69 "Edit section: Male lovers")]

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5f/Cesio%2C_Carlo_1626_-_1686%29_-_Apollo_e_Giacinto%2C_inc._da_Annibale_Carracci%2C_-1675-.jpg/220px-Cesio%2C_Carlo_1626_-_1686%29_-_Apollo_e_Giacinto%2C_inc._da_Annibale_Carracci%2C_-1675-.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Cesio,_Carlo_1626_-_1686\)_-_Apollo_e_Giacinto,_inc._da_Annibale_Carracci,_-1675-.jpg)

*Apollo and Hyacinthus*, by [Carlo Cesio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carlo_Cesio "Carlo Cesio")

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/0f/Kiselev_Death_of_Hyacinth.jpg/220px-Kiselev_Death_of_Hyacinth.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Kiselev_Death_of_Hyacinth.jpg)

Death of Hyacinth, by [Alexander Kiselyov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexander_Kiselyov_\(painter\) "Alexander Kiselyov (painter)"), 1850–1900

Hyacinth (or Hyacinthus), a beautiful and athletic [Spartan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sparta "Sparta") prince, was one of Apollo's favourite lovers.[\[292\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-294) The pair was practicing throwing the [discus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Discus_throw "Discus throw") when a discus thrown by Apollo was blown off course by the jealous [Zephyrus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zephyrus "Zephyrus") and struck Hyacinthus in the head, killing him instantly. Apollo is said to be filled with grief. Out of Hyacinthus' blood, Apollo created a [flower](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hyacinth_\(plant\) "Hyacinth (plant)") named after him as a memorial to his death, and his tears stained the flower petals with the interjection αἰαῖ, meaning *alas*.[\[293\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-295) He was later resurrected and taken to heaven. The festival [Hyacinthia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hyacinthia "Hyacinthia") was a national celebration of Sparta, which commemorated the death and rebirth of Hyacinthus.[\[294\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-296)

Another male lover was [Cyparissus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cyparissus "Cyparissus"), a descendant of [Heracles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heracles "Heracles"). Apollo gave him a tame deer as a companion but Cyparissus accidentally killed it with a [javelin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pilum "Pilum") as it lay asleep in the undergrowth. Cyparissus was so saddened by its death that he asked Apollo to let his tears fall forever. Apollo granted the request by turning him into the [Cypress](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cupressaceae "Cupressaceae") named after him, which was said to be a sad tree because the sap forms droplets like tears on the trunk.[\[295\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-297)

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7e/Granger-Apollo.jpg/200px-Granger-Apollo.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Granger-Apollo.jpg)

Apollo and [Cyparissus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cyparissus "Cyparissus"), by [Jean-Pierre Granger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jean-Pierre_Granger "Jean-Pierre Granger") (1779–1840)

[Admetus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Admetus "Admetus"), the king of Pherae, was also Apollo's lover.[\[296\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-298)[\[297\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Plutarch,_Numa-299) During his exile, which lasted either for one year or nine years,[\[298\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-300) Apollo served Admetus as a herdsman. The romantic nature of their relationship was first described by [Callimachus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Callimachus "Callimachus") of Alexandria, who wrote that Apollo was "fired with love" for Admetus.[\[159\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-ReferenceA-161) Plutarch lists Admetus as one of Apollo's lovers and says that Apollo served Admetus because he doted upon him.[\[299\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-301) Latin poet [Ovid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ovid "Ovid") in his *[Ars Amatoria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ars_Amatoria "Ars Amatoria")* said that even though he was a god, Apollo forsook his pride and stayed in as a servant for the sake of Admetus.[\[300\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-302) [Tibullus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tibullus "Tibullus") describes Apollo's love to the king as *servitium amoris* (slavery of love) and asserts that Apollo became his servant not by force but by choice. He would also make cheese and serve it to Admetus. His domestic actions caused embarrassment to his family.[\[301\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-303)

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/93/Nicolas-Antoine_Taunay_-_Apolo_visitando_Admeto.jpg/250px-Nicolas-Antoine_Taunay_-_Apolo_visitando_Admeto.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Nicolas-Antoine_Taunay_-_Apolo_visitando_Admeto.jpg)

Apollo visiting Admetus, by [Nicolas-Antoine Taunay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nicolas-Antoine_Taunay "Nicolas-Antoine Taunay"), 19th century

> Oh how often his sister (Diana) blushed at meeting her brother as he carried a young calf through the fields!....often Latona lamented when she saw her son's disheveled locks which were admired even by Juno, his step-mother...[\[302\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-304)

When Admetus wanted to marry princess [Alcestis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alcestis "Alcestis"), Apollo provided a chariot pulled by a lion and a boar he had tamed. This satisfied Alcestis' father and he let Admetus marry his daughter. Further, Apollo saved the king from Artemis' wrath and also convinced the [Moirai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moirai "Moirai") to postpone Admetus' death once.\[*[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed "Wikipedia:Citation needed")*]

[Branchus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Branchus_\(lover_of_Apollo\) "Branchus (lover of Apollo)"), a shepherd, one day came across Apollo in the woods. Captivated by the god's beauty, he kissed Apollo. Apollo requited his affections and wanting to reward him, bestowed prophetic skills on him. His descendants, the Branchides, were an influential clan of prophets.[\[303\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-305)

Other male lovers of Apollo include:

- [Adonis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adonis "Adonis"), who is said to have been the lover of both Apollo and Aphrodite. He behaved as a man with Aphrodite and as a woman with Apollo.[\[304\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-306)
- [Atymnius](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atymnius "Atymnius"),[\[305\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-307) otherwise known as a beloved of [Sarpedon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sarpedon_\(brother_of_Minos\) "Sarpedon (brother of Minos)")
- [Boreas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boreas_\(god\) "Boreas (god)"), the god of North winds[\[306\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-308)
- [Cinyras](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cinyras "Cinyras"), king of Cyprus and the priest of Aphrodite[\[307\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-309)
- [Helenus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Helenus "Helenus"), a Trojan prince (son of [Priam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Priam "Priam") and [Hecuba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hecuba "Hecuba")). He received from Apollo an ivory bow with which he later wounded Achilles in the hand.[\[308\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-310)
- Hippolytus of [Sicyon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sicyon "Sicyon") (not the same as [Hippolytus, the son of Theseus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hippolytus_\(mythology\) "Hippolytus (mythology)"))[\[297\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Plutarch,_Numa-299)
- [Hymenaios](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hymenaios "Hymenaios"), the son of [Magnes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Magnes_\(son_of_Argos\) "Magnes (son of Argos)")[\[309\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-311)
- [Iapis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iapis "Iapis"), to whom Apollo taught the art of healing[\[310\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-312)
- [Phorbas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Phorbas_of_Thessaly "Phorbas of Thessaly"), the dragon slayer (probably the son of Triopas)[\[311\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-313)

### Children

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Apollo\&action=edit\&section=70 "Edit section: Children")]

|                                                                                                                                                                            |                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| [![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/9/99/Question_book-new.svg/50px-Question_book-new.svg.png)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Question_book-new.svg) | This section **needs additional citations for [verification](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Verifiability "Wikipedia:Verifiability")**. Please help [improve this article](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special:EditPage/Apollo "Special:EditPage/Apollo") by [adding citations to reliable sources](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:Referencing_for_beginners "Help:Referencing for beginners") in this section. Unsourced material may be challenged and removed. *(December 2024)**&#x20;([Learn how and when to remove this message](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:Maintenance_template_removal "Help:Maintenance template removal"))* |

Apollo sired many children, from mortal women and nymphs as well as the goddesses. His children grew up to be physicians, musicians, poets, seers or archers. Many of his sons founded new cities and became kings.

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/dd/1590._Apollo_Entrusting_Chiron_with_the_Education_of_Aescalapius_-_etching_-_Washington_DC%2C_NGA.jpg/220px-1590._Apollo_Entrusting_Chiron_with_the_Education_of_Aescalapius_-_etching_-_Washington_DC%2C_NGA.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:1590._Apollo_Entrusting_Chiron_with_the_Education_of_Aescalapius_-_etching_-_Washington_DC,_NGA.jpg)

Apollo entrusting [Chiron](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chiron "Chiron") with the education of Aescalapius

[Asclepius](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asclepius "Asclepius") is the most famous son of Apollo. His skills as a physician surpassed that of Apollo's. Zeus killed him for bringing back the dead, but upon Apollo's request, he was resurrected as a god. [Aristaeus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aristaeus "Aristaeus") was placed under the care of Chiron after his birth. He became the god of beekeeping, cheese-making, animal husbandry and more. He was ultimately given immortality for the benefits he bestowed upon humanity. The [Corybantes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Corybantes "Corybantes") were spear-clashing, dancing demigods.

The sons of Apollo who participated in the Trojan War include the Trojan princes [Hector](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hector "Hector") and [Troilus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Troilus "Troilus"), as well as [Tenes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tenes "Tenes"), the king of [Tenedos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tenedos "Tenedos"), all three of whom were killed by Achilles over the course of the war.

Apollo's children who became musicians and bards include [Orpheus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Orpheus "Orpheus"), [Linus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Linus_of_Thrace "Linus of Thrace"), [Ialemus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ialemus "Ialemus"), [Hymenaeus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hymen_\(god\) "Hymen (god)"), [Philammon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philammon "Philammon"), [Eumolpus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eumolpus "Eumolpus") and [Eleuther](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eleuther "Eleuther"). Apollo fathered 3 daughters, [Apollonis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apollonis "Apollonis"), [Borysthenis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Borysthenis "Borysthenis") and [Cephisso](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cephisso "Cephisso"), who formed a group of minor Muses, the "Musa Apollonides".[\[312\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-314) Plutarch recounts that the Delphians believed the three Muses to be Nete, Mese, and Hypate, after the highest, middle, and lowest strings of the lyre.[\[313\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-315) [Phemonoe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Phemonoe "Phemonoe") was a seer and poet who was the inventor of Hexameter.

[Apis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apis_\(Greek_mythology\) "Apis (Greek mythology)"), [Idmon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Idmon "Idmon"), [Iamus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iamus "Iamus"), [Tenerus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tenerus_\(son_of_Apollo\) "Tenerus (son of Apollo)"), [Mopsus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mopsus "Mopsus"), [Galeus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Themisto_\(disambiguation\) "Themisto (disambiguation)"), Telmessus and others were gifted seers. [Anius](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anius "Anius"), Pythaeus and [Ismenus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ismenus "Ismenus") lived as high priests. Most of them were trained by Apollo himself.

[Arabus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arabius_\(mythology\) "Arabius (mythology)"), [Delphos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delphos_\(mythology\) "Delphos (mythology)"), [Dryops](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dryops_\(mythology\) "Dryops (mythology)"), [Miletos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Miletos "Miletos"), [Tenes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tenes "Tenes"), [Epidaurus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Epidaurus_\(mythology\) "Epidaurus (mythology)"), [Ceos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ceos_\(mythology\) "Ceos (mythology)"), [Lycoras](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lycorus "Lycorus"), [Syrus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syrus "Syrus"), [Pisus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pisus "Pisus"), [Marathus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marathon_\(mythology\) "Marathon (mythology)"), [Megarus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Megarus "Megarus"), [Patarus](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Patarus\&action=edit\&redlink=1 "Patarus (page does not exist)"), [Acraepheus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Acraepheus "Acraepheus"), [Cicon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cicon "Cicon"), [Chaeron](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Chaeron_\(mythology\)\&action=edit\&redlink=1 "Chaeron (mythology) (page does not exist)") and many other sons of Apollo, under the guidance of his words, founded eponymous cities.

He also had a son by Agathippe who was named Chrysorrhoas who was a mechanic artist.[\[314\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-316) His other daughters include [Eurynome](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eurynome "Eurynome"), [Chariclo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chariclo "Chariclo") wife of [Chiron](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chiron "Chiron"), [Eurydice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eurydice "Eurydice") the wife of Orpheus, [Eriopis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eriopis "Eriopis"), famous for her beautiful hair, [Melite](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Melite_\(heroine\) "Melite (heroine)") the heroine, [Pamphile](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pamphile "Pamphile") the silk weaver, Parthenos, and by some accounts, Phoebe, Hilyra and [Scylla](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scylla "Scylla"). Apollo turned Parthenos into a constellation after her early death.

Additionally, Apollo fostered and educated [Chiron](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chiron "Chiron"), the centaur who later became the greatest teacher and educated many demigods, including Apollo's sons. Apollo also fostered [Carnus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carnus "Carnus"), the son of [Zeus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zeus "Zeus") and [Europa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Europa_\(consort_of_Zeus\) "Europa (consort of Zeus)").
